# Log

What each run did, newest first. Claude appends a short entry after each development session; the detailed record of every bot action is in the pinned Daily log issue. The "C2 evidence" section below is a standing section: it stays at the top, and dated entries go under it.

## 2026-11-25: ART0: the 1280×720 picture, one rule for art at 2x detail, the curl redrawn

The owner's decision of 2026-11-21, confirmed 2026-11-25 (docs/DECISIONS.md): 64×64 art a 32-unit cell at the same size on screen in a 1280×720 picture; on 1080p a fractional ×1.5 stretch with hard pixels (the owner's choice after seeing 960×540 and 640×360 laid out). The foundation, not the redraw (docs/STYLE.md, "The picture and the art's detail"):

- **The picture:** project.godot 1280×720, integer scaling, places snapped to the picture's pixels; `Display.stretch_for` stretches by a fraction where whole numbers would leave the screen mostly empty (1080p). `PictureGrid` moved to core/display: the cameras show the same world; the HUD (its layer and `Root`), the main menu, the intro, Feel's markers and Y map and the split's dividers are laid out at 640×360 and drawn twice as big. The light, the mist and the air keep their own resolution.
- **Mixed art:** `ArtDetail` (core/display): a texture named `<name>@2x.<ext>` has two art pixels a world unit and is drawn at half size. Sprites, faces (a staff's own `FaceArt`, `StaffBase.face`), the staff's head, enemy bodies, the elite ring, `TexturePage` regions, the cover's batches and the floor bake (baked at the picture's scale, on a sprite of its own) take it.
- **The pilot:** the curl staff's head (16 directions × 4 ramps, 96×96 frames) and the Optimist's eyes and brows at 2x, drawn by `tools/staff_art.py`; the golden player is `art/kit/golden_staff@2x.svg`, the kit has `grid_64.svg`; art/README.md says how to redraw a sprite at 64×64.
- **The same game:** the state hash every tick equal to main's (tick_hash.gd, 1p and 4p, seed 11); the determinism, snapshot and content-determinism tests green. Tests that read the cameras' zoom or a body sprite's scale read them through the picture's scale and the art's detail.

## 2026-11-23: A steady camera: zoom 0.8, no framing of foes, foe markers

The owner's decision: "I think the dynamic camera, where it tries to fit nearby enemies into view, should be discarded again. It makes the cameras jump around. Maybe zoom a bit more out as the default instead. And then maybe we can have edge markers, that points to enemies out of view in the room." Their answers: default zoom 0.8 everywhere, the close-foes zoom removed entirely, markers in sealed fights only (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "A steady camera"; docs/DECISIONS.md). This reverses the entry of 2026-11-19 below ("The camera holds close foes").

Done, all in Feel: `close_foes.gd` and what served it are deleted (the party camera's framed box, the split views' `zoom_for_foes`); the party camera's `MAX_ZOOM` is 0.8 and the split's `SPLIT_ZOOM` is that constant; a split view keeps the floor that never shows past the camera's limits (`view_zoom_in`). New: `foe_markers.gd`, small Fire-coloured triangles at a view's edge for a sealed fight's foes out of it, growing in and shrinking out over three looks, at most six a view and two more for bosses, never piled up, always on their own view's edge (a review measured the first build's sliding marker in another view's cell on a split and across the picture on a merge, and doubled markers on a regroup: a marker now follows its edge, shrinks out and grows in anew where its place leaps, and is handed over between views that want it on one spot; views have ids that stay theirs, `ScreenViews.ids`); the cell-edge geometry it shares with the teammate markers moved to `ScreenViews.reach_inside`. The old close-foes tests went (no camera takes foes any more; Feel's own step is checked not to move its camera for a fight's foe), the merge-time test's fight cases are plain cases, and `test_foe_markers.gd` and `test_foe_markers_split.gd` are new.

The screenshot script (`shot_view_framing.gd`) was run and looked at: the fight at 0.8 with the marker at the picture's edge, and a split case with the marker in its seat's cell; the marker was lengthened from 9 to 12 px after a reviewer missed it. Not done here: the frame times at 0.8 were not measured (every view draws 1.56 times the world area at rest), and world-space text at 0.8 was not in any shot. The owner's playtest decides the marker's look and the numbers marked "decided while building".

## 2026-11-23: Damage numbers from one sheet

The owner's question ("I dont understand why performance is so much worse, when eg. 4 players are playing locally. Is there something we are doing 4 times, that could be just 1 time?") and choice ("Do the ones, with most bang for the buck. I think drawing makes sense?"). Measured first on the owner's GPU (the Web Test export in Chrome, the shipped benchmark's web rows, the fixed clock; frame / sim / Feel ms, draw calls): 1p near 8.1 / 1.4 / 3.3, 44; 1p far 13.4 / 3.1 / 4.4, 114; 2p apart dynamic 20.8 / 5.4 / 5.9, 260; 4p apart equal 37.6 / 10.5 / 9.2, 541. So with four players apart the tick is about 28% of the frame, Feel 24% and the drawing the rest, and a view costs about a one-player picture. A draw census by part of the four views (`play_frames.gd --census --census-calls`, the web's renderer on llvmpipe): the damage numbers 144 of 465 draw calls, the actors and their faces about 110, the motes' chunks 41. `--census` now also tells the motes' rows drawn against those alive (`MoteRegions.rows_drawn`, `rows_alive`): on the desktop's ring 18 to 31% of the 33,000 rows drawn with four players are alive (the web's ring: 10,000 drawn, 55% alive); not yet acted on.

Built: the damage numbers are drawn from a sheet a font size (`gameplay/feel/number_glyphs.gd`; gameplay/feel/README.md, "Damage numbers"), one draw call a run of numbers of one size (every number today) where a run was two and a number over another started a new run. Draw calls a frame (`play_frames.gd`, the web's renderer on llvmpipe, one run each): 4p apart equal web 536 to 374, 1p far web 114 to 103; primitives the same. The same pixels as the font's drawing with each glyph on a whole pixel (`tests/screenshots/shot_number_glyphs.gd`: within 2 of 255 on both renderers). Not yet measured on the GPU.

## 2026-11-22: test_net_rtc's DTLS flake: a handshake its close cut short

The owner asked for `tests/net/test_net_rtc.gd` to be made reliable: it failed three times today in full `tools/ptest.sh` runs, in its batch, and passed alone. What the three reports held (from the sessions that ran them): no check failed; the file failed for two engine errors alone, `DTLS recv: Handshake failed: SSL - The connection indicated an EOF` and `DTLS handshake failed` (the runner fails a file on any `ERROR:` line), once as its batch's first file. In libdatachannel 0.24.6 (webrtc-native 1.2.2) that EOF is a DTLS transport stopped while its handshake is under way.

The cause: `test_a_code_of_the_wrong_side_or_no_code_is_refused_and_another_may_be_given` gave its second joining machine (`other_client`) the second host's real code, to check that a host's code is taken after a joining machine's was refused; the two are never linked and are closed at the test's end. But a machine answers a connectivity check before it has the other's code (RFC 8445 7.3; libjuice does), so the joining machine's ICE connects and it begins a DTLS handshake the host never answers, which the close then stops. Whether that stop is logged is a race inside libdatachannel: its teardown thread stops the transport and posts one last read to its thread pool, then frees it; when a pool worker on another core runs that read first, it reads the EOF and logs both lines. An idle machine, or one core (tried first here, 0 errors in 90 runs), lets the teardown win; a loaded machine with several cores does not always. Found by a review's unanchored look after about 130 loaded runs of the file did not fail; then shown with the step alone repeated, 40 times a process, 4 processes next to 8 busy loops: with the real code 16 errors in 1,440 closes (8 of 36 processes), with its addresses hidden 0 in 1,440 (0 of 36).

The fix: that joining machine takes the host's code with its addresses hidden (`_unreachable`, as the two time-out tests already do for the same reason). The check is unchanged (a host's code is taken), and every other peer the file makes is either linked on both ends before it is closed (both channels open: both handshakes done) or never knows the other's address. The guard (the owner's answer on #921): every handshake the file makes comes from `_rtc()`, and after each test one never linked must hold no real address of another (`NetRtc.their_code()`, read only; a candidate line in a code's description counts too), else a check fails, every run with the WebRTC extension, with no race needed; with the old step put back it fails that test, and a test checks that no `NetRtc` is made outside `_rtc()`. `tools/ptest_failures.py` now also says, under each `ERROR:` line, where in the suite it was logged (`during:` the first test to end after it, or the end of the one before, since a thread may log what a test's close began after that test's end; before or after the suite), and cuts its list by whole reasons, so a failure of this kind names its test next time.

## 2026-11-22: The effects on threads

The owner's decision of 2026-11-22: Feel's per-frame effects on worker threads on the desktop. `FeelJobs` (gameplay/feel/feel_jobs.gd) runs independent pieces as `WorkerThreadPool` group tasks: the light grid a view a piece (the light kernel's new view range), the rooms' mists a room a piece when four or more are stepped. The same frames either way (a held frame threaded and on one thread, 0 px differ; the arrays byte for byte in `test_feel_jobs.gd`); the web and `Feel.set_effect_threads(false)` keep one thread. On this 4-core container, where llvmpipe draws on the same cores, the threads do not make frames faster (2p apart Dynamic 3 to 5 ms slower threaded); the separate render thread is much faster but logs renderer errors, so it stays off (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "The effects on threads").

## 2026-11-22: One pass

The owner's second step after grid lighting (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "One pass"). Measured first at 1280 x 720: once the light is a grid, #902's passes cost more than they save, so the light's factor passes and the mist's pass are gone and both are worked out a world pixel at a time where they are drawn; the apply rect is the one full-screen pass (light, ambient, tints, one screen read). The mist stays its own draw under the bodies: a ground mask in the screen's alpha would merge it, but Vulkan's screen copy keeps no alpha (open with the owner). A table of the effects' cost (`EffectsQuality`, LOW and HIGH: the light's cell and smoothing, the mists' billows, the air's share) for the coming quality setting, `Spectacle.set_effects_quality`.

## 2026-11-22: The desktop's native module optimised (D2)

The owner's decision of 2026-11-22: the game is optimised for its Windows and Linux builds first, so build the native module for the desktop with stronger optimisation, bit-exact with its GDScript and the same on every machine. godot-cpp's release target was already `-O3`. What changed (`native/SConstruct`): the desktop libraries are linked whole (LTO, `lto=full`, with the float flags passed to the link too) and built for `x86-64-v2` (SSE4.2; Godot 4.7 will not start without it) in place of plain x86-64. The web is built as before. `ci/build/native.sh` now refuses a Linux or Windows library that holds a fused multiply-add instruction or calls the C library's `fma` (`no_fma`), so a flag, compiler or intrinsic that slips past `-ffp-contract=off` fails the build. `x86-64-v3` (AVX2) was built and measured too. It was no faster than v2 and often slower, and Godot has no CPU feature tag to pick a second library by, so there is no AVX2 library.

The same game: the four whole biomes' state hash (`tick_hash.gd --sturdy --hash-every=1`), main's library and this one, is identical every tick: 1p seed 11 (12,087 ticks), 2p seed 23 (18,417), 3p seed 37 (17,109) and 4p seed 11 (14,280). The kernels' side-by-side tests (`test_native_kernel.gd`, `test_native_motes.gd`, `test_wall_slide.gd`, `test_light_grid.gd`, `test_dmath.gd`, `test_projectiles_step_exact.gd`) and the determinism and snapshot tests pass with the v2 library, and with the v3 one too. No fast-math, no `-Ofast`, and no FMA in any build.

Measured, headless, native on 3 threads, this 4-core container, medians of 3 taken in turn (main → v2+LTO, the shipped build → v3+LTO), ms:

| measure | main | v2 + LTO | v3 + LTO |
|---|---|---|---|
| shot_scale storm 30k, hit pass in the kernel | 3.11 | 2.64 | 2.80 |
| shot_scale crowd 30k, in the kernel | 2.20 | 1.91 | 2.10 |
| shot_scale crowd_all 30k, in the kernel | 7.78 | 6.54 | 7.14 |
| shot_scale crowd_all 100k, in the kernel | 19.02 | 16.55 | 16.80 |
| shot_scale crowd_all 100k, update_draw | 7.90 | 6.95 | 7.07 |
| shot_scale crowd_all 100k, manager step | 69.4 | 65.2 | 68.6 |
| shot_scale storm 100k, common pass | 0.365 | 0.378 | 0.363 |
| stress desktop, the hit pass's kernel a tick | 2.01 | 1.73 | 1.87 |
| stress desktop, the tick | 26.7 | 25.8 | 27.6 |
| mote_write shed, us a mote | 2.75 | 2.71 | 2.75 |
| mote_write burst, us a mote | 0.566 | 0.566 | 0.668 |
| the light grid (test_light_grid's scene, 6 kinds of rows and a beam), 8 px / 16 px cells | 1.36 / 0.58 | 1.43 / 0.57 | - |

So the hit pass's kernel is about 13 to 16% faster and the draw writing about 12% at 100k shots. The motes and the common pass are unchanged within the noise, and the light grid at 8 px read 5% slower (1.36 → 1.43; best runs 1.23 → 1.28), likely noise. The play frames (xvfb, llvmpipe, the fixed clock, desktop load, medians of 3 taken in turn, frame / sim / Feel ms, main → this): 1p near 26.9 / 1.31 / 2.86 → 26.5 / 1.17 / 2.78; 1p far 36.7 / 2.65 / 3.79 → 32.0 / 2.30 / 3.58; 4p apart Equal 95.3 / 8.25 / 8.13 → 87.6 / 8.09 / 7.79. At a run's load the kernels are a small part of a frame, so read these as no worse, not as the change's size. The Windows DLL was cross-built here with MinGW (LTO, v2, no runtime DLLs, no FMA) but not run.

## 2026-11-21: Grid lighting

The owner's choice after #902: grid lighting, then one pass, so the light stays cheap at 1280 x 720. The spell light is now worked out on the CPU on a grid of world cells (8 px by default; 12 and 16 for a quality setting), a grid a view in one small texture, its cells on the world's grid so it never swims (`gameplay/feel/light_grid.gd`), in the native module (`MoteKernel.light_grid_*`, native/src/light_grid.cpp, byte for byte with the GDScript in `test_light_grid.gd`), from the same rows the GPU's light read: shots, runes, flashes, cues, spots, carried lights and beams, with the same falloff, colours and blocking. The light buffers (a SubViewport a view), their batches and mirrors, `light.gdshader` and `light_beam.gdshader` are gone; every reader takes the cell its world point lies in (one sampler where there were four). On llvmpipe on the fixed clock (frame ms, main then grid): 640 x 360 start 26.5 to 26.0, near 28.0 to 26.2, far 40.6 to 39.5, two views 65.0 to 61.1, four 106.0 to 104.6; 1280 x 720 52.5 to 52.0, 61.1 to 61.1, 74.0 to 72.9, 110.0 to 103.6, 167.1 to 162.6. What 1280 x 720 still doubles is the root's fill, the one pass's next. Read whole, the cells are blockier than the buffer's linear light; read smoothed (`light_grid_smooth`) they look like before. Screenshots on `shots/perf/grid-light`. Details: docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Grid lighting".

The owner's answers on #907: smoothed 8 px cells by default (whole cells an option), every light reaches at least one cell (a test), and the split's cheaper light rung is 16 px cells. The `LightKernel` split was allowed but this session's permission check refused the `ci/smbot/rules.py` line, so the methods stay on `MoteKernel`.

## 2026-11-21: The tick and hidden cover views a little cheaper, the game the same

The owner's choice for this round's CPU work, part C (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "The tick cheaper, the game the same"): hidden rooms' cover views idle more, and the simulation's tick cheaper, the simulation exactly the same. Profiled first with a new benchmark, `tests/benchmark/tick_hash.gd` (the bots' playtest over a whole crypt biome, `--profile-steps` timing each of the Tick's steps from a second into the run, the biome's lay left out, and `--hash-every=1` writing the whole state hash every tick). Steady play, 1p: the Tick 1.10 ms a tick native, Flow 0.34 (of it the rooms' waking 0.18), the bodies 0.25 (the wizard's think, caster and move; the foes' AI), Cover 0.17 (bodies looked at for cobwebs at rest elsewhere), Projectiles 0.12, Spells 0.07, Rewards 0.06 (every coin lying times every seat).

Done, each exactly the same game: the waking looks each body up once and sets every body's sleep in one call (`Actors.sleep_with_places`), asking no body whether it keeps a place awake that already is; Cover looks into no room none of whose cells harms now (`CoverRoom.harms_nobody`), steps no room at rest and passes a sleeping body nobody harms before looking it up; Rewards finds the wizards' feet once for every coin (again after a take); the map's teleports read the cleared rooms as one mask (`RoomLife.rooms_with`). Feel: a cover view switched off walks its cells straight from the bodies found, by each body's reach (the box of the 32 nearest spans most of a biome), takes the bodies and sends its walk times only when shown again, and a room's fires far out of the picture roll no motes (only their order of dice differs, the view's). The state hash is the same every tick as main's over four whole biomes won by bots: 1p seed 11 (12,087 ticks), 2p seed 23 (18,417), 3p seed 37 (17,109) and 4p seed 11 (14,280).

Measured, headless, native, the bots' biome of seed 11, main and this run side by side (ms a tick): 1p **1.10 → 0.99** (Flow 0.34 → 0.31, Cover 0.17 → 0.13, Rewards 0.061 → 0.030, the bodies 0.25 → 0.25, Projectiles 0.12 → 0.12); 4p **1.61 → 1.43** (the bodies 0.52 → 0.51, Flow 0.37 → 0.33, Cover 0.19 → 0.16, Rewards 0.115 → 0.039). Feel's other cover views, 1p, headless: 0.58 → about 0.48 ms a frame. The play frames (xvfb, llvmpipe, the fixed clock, medians of 3, frame / sim / Feel ms; main → this): 1p start web 25.0 / 1.11 / 2.92 → 25.3 / 1.11 / 3.18; 1p near web 33.8 / 1.57 / 3.43 → 27.8 / 1.44 / 3.28; 1p far web 41.7 / 2.94 / 4.20 → 42.1 / 3.22 / 4.77; 2p apart Dynamic 64.2 / 5.21 / 5.93 → 63.4 / 5.11 / 5.92; 4p apart Equal 109.3 / 9.79 / 9.50 → 112.6 / 9.98 / 9.74. At this size the frames' spread (runs taken hours apart on a shared machine) hides the change; the headless profile shows it. What is left is spread thin: no step's loop is worth a native kernel yet; the bodies' cost is each wizard's think, cast and move.

## 2026-11-21: Baked floors; an enemy and a candle one draw call each

The owner's choice after #900 (round two of the draw calls, part A), for the web build with one player first. Three wins, each guarded by a structural test:

- **A room's floor in one call, not seven** (`gameplay/feel/ground/floor_bake.gd`): once a room's ground is drawn, its floor, rock face, glow and its walls' shades are drawn once into a viewport of the floor's size, in the screen's order (the glow added by `floor_bake.gdshader`, which keeps the texture premultiplied, so over a pit's clear pixels the glow is only light), and from the next frame the `Floor` shows that texture and the four layers are hidden and let go. Memory, a 16-room crypt biome with its 14 walkways: about 19 MB of baked floors (rooms 1.0 MB each on average, walkways 0.2), in place of the drawn floors, rock faces and glows (about 43 MB). `test_floor_bake.gd`.
- **An enemy's body and face on one page a kind** (`TexturePage`, core/base; `CreatureLook` and `Face.wear_sheets`): the body, its poses' strips and the eye and brow sheets copied side by side at a kind's first spawn. `test_enemy_page.gd`, `tests/core/test_texture_page.gd`.
- **A candle's wax and flame on one page** (`gameplay/feel/decal_pages.gd`, the torch's bracket and flame too): built at load from the existing art, the flame's frames a copy with the same frames, durations, speed and loop, playing on from its frame. `test_decal_pages.gd`.

The same pixels, held against the old drawing in one frozen frame (`tests/screenshots/shot_baked_floors.gd`: the enemies, the candles, the floor, its rock face and glow pixel for pixel), but where something lies between the layers on screen: ground cover, a floor mark, the floor mist or a shot on the floor now lies over a wall's shade and the glow, not under (a cobweb under a sarcophagus's shade is no longer darkened: up to 45 of 255 on its pixels; the mist over the shades, 1 to 4; round a pit under the shaft's light, up to 9). Medians of 3, main (#902) then this branch, alternating (play_frames on the fixed clock of #901, llvmpipe on this 4-core container):

| scenario | draw calls | primitives | Feel ms | sim ms | frame ms |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1p start web | 63 → 54 | 5653 → 5632 | 2.67 → 2.51 | 1.02 → 0.94 | 20.6 → 19.5 |
| 1p near web | 86 → 70 | 5625 → 5611 | 2.70 → 2.77 | 1.26 → 1.27 | 22.6 → 21.1 |
| 1p far web | 158 → 138 | 18760 → 18707 | 3.95 → 3.72 | 2.52 → 2.44 | 31.8 → 29.0 |
| 2p apart dynamic | 381 → 344 * | 56740 → 56678 * | 5.25 → 3.58 | 4.31 → 4.01 | 50.2 → 23.3 |
| 4p apart equal | 787 → 718 | 115030 → 114439 | 7.68 → 7.97 | 7.81 → 7.97 | 82.9 → 81.8 |

The counts are the same in every run of a scenario, but for 2p apart dynamic: there two of the branch's three runs framed the two wizards in one view (75 calls, 6.2 k primitives; main did so once in an earlier round on the real clock, the camera variance #901 notes), so * is the one run framed as main's (its median Feel, sim and frame ms are of the one-view runs). Primitives do not change (an earlier round on the real clock seemed to show a rise: it was the scenarios' variance). Feel adds nothing a frame (the bake's step walks an empty list once every floor is shown); its milliseconds and the simulation's move within run-to-run noise. ## 2026-11-21: The split's divider goes in time once the players are together

The owner's report: "Sometimes when local players go back near each other, the voronoi line split does not disappear for some time." Measured with wizards walking back at a wizard's pace (`tests/gameplay/feel/test_split_merge_time.gd`): a merging view went only once its picture was within 0.05 px of the one it eased onto, which was itself easing, so the divider stayed 2.4 s once players stood together (2.8 s in a fight) and for good while they walked on together or foes kept the party camera moving. Built: a merging view lands on its target over `MERGE_TIME` (0.3 s, a smoothstep from rest, however the target moves), and its anchor walks onto its target's at 12 px a frame (`MERGE_ANCHOR_STEP`). After: one picture 0.32 s after the split's end in every case (2 to 4 players, fights, walking on; 0.4 s on equal panes with four); a pair beside a far player has no divider between them within 0.32 s of standing within `JOIN_REACH`. No flicker at the fit or at `JOIN_REACH` (the hysteresis kept). View only (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "The merge ends in time"). Also, from the owner's ask on #901 (benchmark runs framing differently): some runs had no current camera, since a new world camera made at the address of a node freed earlier in the same processing pass was skipped by its own `make_current` (Godot skips nodes that left a group during a pass or group call, by address); Flow makes it current again before the frame is drawn (`Flow._make_current`), with `test_camera_current.gd` and `test_fixed_split_frames.gd`.

## 2026-11-21: Each corner panel drawn once

Its see-through backing stays drawn live, and what moves most frames of a fight (each staff row's glyph shade and rim and its mana line) is drawn over the picture by one live layer of plain rects, so a panel is three draw calls idle or fighting (about 25 before with four staffs). A staff row, the meter line and the quick-use cross now redraw only when their pixels change (a cooldown's shade moves a pixel every few frames, not every frame). The picture is the screen's size with the content at the panel's own place: a picture the panel's size picked other texels of the card icons, which are scaled down onto texel edges. Over a fixed backdrop the panels match main in every state shot (mid-cooldown, the D-pad bound and recharging, the next-cast style, four corners) but for about 3,000 see-through pixels over four panels, by at most 2 in 255 (premultiplied rounding).

## 2026-11-21: Fewer draw calls and less of Feel a frame

The owner's ask: "find more ways to bring down the frame time ... reduce draw calls or something else", for the web build with one player first. Profiled first with a new draw census (`tests/benchmark/draw_census.gd`: `--census` on the play and biome frames, and `--census-calls`, which pauses the tree and hides each part in turn to tell its draw calls) and the tree profile. One wizard idle in a fight room drew 98 draw calls: the world's actors about 3 each (the body and the face's two sheets), each candle 2, a room's shades 4 and floor 3, and the HUD's one corner panel 36 (a staff row's strip up to 9: fill, icon, frame card by card, an icon a texture); in a fight 15 foes' faces 30 and the damage numbers 16. On the CPU Feel's step was 5 to 6.5 ms a frame on this machine (native; several times that in WASM): every room's cover view handed its shader the clock and the bodies though all but the few in view are switched off (0.93 ms), and the shut Y map settled every piece's look each frame (0.5 ms).

Done, each pixel for pixel the same in the screenshots that show it: a staff row draws its fills, then its icons (copied once onto one page, `IconAtlas`), then its lines, three runs; a face's eyes and brows are one page of the shared sheets; damage numbers are drawn in runs (every outline, then every glyph, 16 at most) of numbers whose boxes do not meet; a cover view switched off hands its batches nothing until shown again; the shut map settles its looks when read or opened. Medians of 3, main then this (llvmpipe, alternating runs): 1p near web 98 to 86 draw calls, Feel 5.03 to 4.60 ms; 1p start web 74 to 62, 4.73 to 4.37 ms; 1p far web 180 to 159, 6.25 to 5.01 ms; 2p apart dynamic 316 to 304. On llvmpipe the frame is the rasteriser's and moves little (35.7 to 35.8, 31.3 to 30.4, 51.7 to 49.4 ms); on WebGL a draw call and the GDScript cost more. No change to the simulation.

The owner's decision: unvisited rooms should not be black, they should look like the background round the rooms (the void) with its motes drifting about, until revealed (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Unvisited rooms are the void"). The dark of an unvisited room was already the void's colour; what told it apart was that the void's motes stopped at its box, being given off only outside every place and drawn under the dark. Now the void's motes have a pool of their own (`Spectacle.void_pool`, the void's kind alone, VOID_SHARE of the ring as before), drawn at `VOID_MOTE_Z` (21), over the dark (20) and under the labels (22), and are given off over the rectangles the dark covers whole as over the void (`UnseenDark.void_covers`, handed on by Feel each frame, the same array; `AirMotes.is_void`): an unvisited room and a hidden secret room's bridge drift with the void, and a mote crosses a room's edge without stopping. The room's own motes stay in the motes' pool, under the dark. The reveal is #884's, unchanged; from its first stage the room is the void no more: no void motes are given off over it, and its chunks of the void's pool drop to the motes' z, so those already there go under the opening blocks and stay under its telegraphs (the review's). Motes by room (#892) hold: the void's pool is drawn by room too, so the void's motes over a hidden room are in that room's chunk of the void's pool, never the motes' pool's. View only. ## 2026-11-21: Three ways into a secret room

The owner's decision of 2026-11-20 (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Three ways into a secret room"): a secret room is reached by a hidden bridge, wall to wall, or a secret rune, the three mixed in a biome. Each secret room's way is rolled as the biome is made, by the run's rules (`MapRules.secret_ways`, the crypt's even), under a purpose of its own. Wall to wall places the room against another's wall, their rectangles touching, joined by a seam (a 64 px doorway of the rooms' own flagstones, two new walkways that are never a bridge across the void); its cracked wall stands in the doorway and opens as the hidden bridge does. A rune joins the rooms with no piece (`BiomeMap.add_rune`): a faint crack in the host room's floor, found by the secret room's element as a rock is, then teleporting a wizard standing on it to the secret room and back (`SecretRunes`, `rune_looks.gd`). The Y map now draws a secret room only once found, whichever way (a room only shown by `map_reveal` was drawn before). Over crypt seeds 1 to 60: 37 hidden bridges, 32 wall to wall, 24 runes, every map keeping the generator's rules. Two follow-ups from #895: the crypt's styles with no loop pull lean toward placements that leave doorways for extra entrances (`MapStyle.entrance_pull`; over seeds 1 to 200, 136 bridges past a tree in 70 maps against 103 in 45), and on an open Y map a room only known through a bridge comes in by stages of its pixels rather than in one step. The four-bot smoke test wins seed 11 as pinned.

## 2026-11-20: No dead ends: secret bridges hidden, extra entrances, doorways off-centre

The owner's report "Some of the rooms seem to have bridges with dead ends", their answer "rooms can have multiple entrances, if it makes sense in the layout", and their two decisions after the first screenshots: the bridge to a secret room is hidden until the secret is found, and doorways may stand anywhere along an edge (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "No dead ends, and extra entrances"). Swept over crypt seeds 1 to 200, the generator made no dead end: every bridge meets two rooms' sockets in the map, in the drawn ground and in a world laid by the game, and every socket without a bridge stays lip. The dead ends were the secret rooms' bridges, and the held Y map drawing a bridge without its room. A secret room's bridge is now pure void until its rock breaks, the rock standing in the doorway of the room the bridge leaves; broken, the bridge builds out toward the secret room band by band. The map draws a bridge's far room in shadow. The generator gained step 5b: in every style, two free doorways of two rooms a walkway spans clear are joined wherever the rules still hold (no roll; never a secret room). `arena_01`, `arena_06` and `arena_08` got off-centre doorways. Bridges past a tree's over 200 maps: 68 in 33 before, 103 in 45 now. The four-bot smoke test wins seed 11 as pinned.

## 2026-11-20: Wall-clock budgets that hold under load

The owner's decision after `test_ground_layout.gd` took 155 ms against its 150 ms budget once under a full `tools/ptest.sh` (tests/README.md, "Tests that time the machine"). Every test that asserted a wall-time budget now times through `tests/support/wall_clock.gd`: the work's fastest of a few runs in units of a reference workload (interpreted GDScript, or the engine's C++ on one thread) run in turn with it for as long as it took, so a slow or busy machine slows both alike, against the test's usual multiple times 1.5 (the native kernels on one thread, as the references are; a usual measured on another kind of machine is moved with its numbers). `test_projectiles_kit.gd` (1,500 shots, 120 steps, the kernel on one thread: 64-69 GDScript references, 108 without the kernel; it was 8 ms a step against about 2.2), `test_snapshot.gd` (a restore that lays a biome again: about 6; it was under a second against 23 ms) and `test_ground_layout.gd` (each room's first lay of a fresh layout at most 24 native references, about 6 ms each here, the largest 7; every room on one thread 22-23 references a megapixel). With the code slowed 2× by hand (each timed call padded by its own time) all three fail; with 8 busy loops and 6 of these test processes on 4 cores all pass, the multiples moving by under 20% while the milliseconds tripled (arena_09's lay 155 ms, 7.5 references). Nothing in the survey explains `test_ai_revive.gd`'s one flake: the brain reads no clock, and its behaviours keep no state of their own; #889's trace stays the way to catch it.

## 2026-11-20: Motes by room; light readers shared by the first two views

The owner's two follow-ups to #890 (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Motes by room, readers shared by two views"). In a biome the motes' ring draws nothing: each step copies the rows written into a chunk of the room each mote flies in (`MoteRegions`, gameplay/feel/mote_regions.gd; in `MoteKernel.region_rows` too, to the bit), boxed to the room grown by 96 px, its whole flight inside; a mote at a mouth, in the void or flung past that is a crosser, boxed to the world; a row the ring overwrites has its copy folded away. #890's cull in the motes' vertex shader is gone. The first two split views now share each light reader, its shader picking the view by the canvas transform and size it is drawn through; copies only for the third and fourth views. No change to the simulation or to the ring's rows.

Feel's "camera and split" with four views 2.33 to 2.42 → 2.08 to 2.31 ms. Between runs of the same build the numbers move by up to a factor of four on llvmpipe, where a view's time is mostly filling pixels, so no gain or loss shows here beyond that noise; the owner's GPU and web runs are the measure. ## 2026-11-20: The bots start in the hub and take normal staffs from its rack

The owner's decision after #886, and their answers on #891 (tests/bots/README.md, "The hub"; content/items/README.md). The hub's rack gets three normal premade staffs beside the crazy ones, each on a starter staff with its own test: the Firebolt (fire on a bolt), the Frost seeker (a homing frost bolt) and the Twin bolt (a bolt split in two). The smoke runner, the sweep and the playtest record the hub instead of Play. Each bot plays a hub errand (`tests/bots/bot_hub.gd`):
- It draws its build from the hub's dice.
- It takes its rack staff with A as a player does: the mender the Firebolt, P1 the Frost seeker, P3 the Twin bolt.
- It sets its build and walks to its own corner of the teleporter stone.
- P1 presses A once the stone is lit.

The run from the stone takes the whole player along, and the runners check that each bot carried its build in. The mender holds its heal on RB, a dash on RT and the Firebolt on LB. A fighter holds a dash on RB, its drawn attack on RT and its rack staff on LB. The brain picks the staff by what each one casts next. Bots never take a dev staff or the Wild star. No game rule changed: the content added is the rack's three staffs.

The replay seed now deals the hub's seed first and the run's second, so every replay seed's run is a new map. The comparison is therefore against a control on the same maps: the hub start with the old heal-and-attack mender and no rack staffs.

| | Control | This change |
|---|---|---|
| Sweep 1-60, seeds lost | 1 (42) | 1 (16) |
| Fights cleared on seed 11, 1 / 2 / 4 bots | 2/9, 2/9, 8/9 | 5/9, 8/9, 8/9 |
| Downs by bot, summed | P1 6, P2 6, P3 3, P4 3 | P1 3, P2 10, P3 1, P4 2 |

The mender's downs rise because the runs go further. On main (the Play start, other maps), the sweep lost 2 (10, 31). The pinned seed 11 still wins on its new map. ## 2026-11-20: The recharge travels with the relic; a use is kept by default

The owner's follow-up to #882 (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Quick-use", third round). An `ItemUse` is kept unless it says otherwise (the field's default, the one place; a potion's drink says consumed), and the item validator refuses a relic use that is consumed and a kept use with a cooldown of 0, naming the item and the fix. The cooldown left is now the item's own (`ItemStack.recharge_left`), not its holder's: the inventory steps the stacks it holds, the Items area those on the ground, so a dropped relic keeps recharging and is picked up mid-recharge, its new holder's HUD told; a run's start begins ready, and a rejoin keeps it (Flow's `LeftPlayers`). ## 2026-11-20: Per-room batches, per-view culling, views named by a uniform

The owner's three next performance steps after #885 (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Per-room batches, per-view culling"). In a biome a view draws only the rooms it shows: each shot batch hands its rows to a batch a room boxed to its room (`DrawRegions`, in the native kernel too), the ground cover's batches take their room's box, and the floor marks have a layer a room (no 4,096 px cap: a wide biome's far rooms keep their marks). The motes, one ring written in time order, are culled per view in their shader instead, by the box each view draws. The mote sources, the air and the shadows work by each view's own box, so the split's air is as thick as one picture's. What reads the light is drawn once a view with its view's copy of the material, naming its light buffer by a uniform (an instance uniform hit the compatibility renderer's 256-item limit in a four-way split). No change to the simulation.

Measured (`biome_frames.gd` under xvfb on llvmpipe, this container, main then this branch; the views' render time a frame, then the frame's average): seed 11 desktop Dynamic 73.0 → 70.3 ms (frame 237 → 242), desktop Equal 83.6 → 63.5 ms (frame 283 → 233; main's run had slow outliers, p95 819 → 284), seed 23 web Equal 59.1 → 54.1 ms (frame 160 → 161); the one picture 210 → 214 ms. Seed 11 web Dynamic did not split on main's run, so it has no pair (this branch: views 53.6 ms). Feel's "camera and split" rose from 1.8 to 3.4 ms a split frame, the readers' copies kept in step. On llvmpipe a view's time is mostly filling pixels, not shots' and motes' vertices, so these gains are small; a GPU, and the web, should gain more (to be measured by the owner). Tests `test_draw_regions.gd`, `test_view_boxes.gd`, and new cases in `test_marks.gd`, `test_split_screen.gd`, `test_cover_look.gd`; screenshots `shot_wide_marks.gd`.

## 2026-11-20: Cleared doorway signs; a finer, quicker reveal of unseen rooms

The owner's playtest polish on #883 (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Cleared doorway signs, a finer reveal"). Once a fight, challenge or boss room is cleared, every doorway sign into it and its symbol on the Y map step over seven frames to a calm cleared look: the symbol dimmed to its shadow and a green check drawn across it a pixel a frame, the sign's light dimming with it. Flow tells the view which rooms are cleared in a new event, `rooms_cleared`, read from the room life it already keeps (again after a restore, drawn as it stands). An unseen room now opens by 8 px blocks at 24 fps instead of 16 px at 12, so it opens in about 0.17 s and no longer reads as a grid of black lines mid-way. View only: the simulation keeps no new state. Tests in `test_party_map_view.gd`, `test_biome_sound_and_look.gd` and `test_events.gd`; screenshots `shot_cleared_signs.gd`.

## 2026-11-20: Quick-use: usable items, the cross always shown, 0 left on the ring; active relics

The owner answered #879's questions and then #882's (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Quick-use: usable items, the cross always shown, 0 left on the ring"). An item is usable exactly when it has a use (`ItemContent.use_of()`, an `ItemUse`: what using it does, whether it is consumed, its cooldown); binding, auto-bind, quick-use and the inventory's Use all ask that one question instead of the item's kind, and a test plays every item. The content validator checks each use and names the item and the fix. Relics can have a use too: an active relic is kept by a use, its stat changes staying, and recharges for its cooldown (inventory state, hashed and snapshotted); a press while it recharges is refused, and the corner panel's cross and the inventory's ring show the recharge as a shutter sinking a row at a time. Potions stay consumed. Each corner panel's d-pad cross always shows, its arms empty framed cells until something is bound; the staff rows keep their room and the panel grows by the cross. A bound item with none left stays on the inventory's ring, greyed with a 0, in its place, where its arm lets it go or another arm moves it. The keys 1-4, the auto-bind, no default bindings and the cheat code are as they were; no command changed (one new event). Screenshots: the empty cross, a greyed 0-count potion on the ring, a recharging relic on the cross.

## 2026-11-20: Stronger bot builds and a mend bot

The owner decided on #883 that the test bots get more powerful staffs and that a bot can carry a mend staff to heal its allies (tests/bots/README.md). Each fighter's starting build is now drawn from a small table of the bots' own (`tests/bots/bot_builds.gd`). The draw uses the run's dice (`Sim.chance_int`, purpose `bot_build`, keyed by seat), so the same seed gives the same builds. Every fighter build is a dash on RB and a strong RT: a homing bigger bolt, a homing split bolt, a faster overcharged bolt or a beam. In a team of two or more, P2 is the mender: mend on an overcharged, faster bolt (RB) and a homing bigger bolt (RT). The owner allowed it a third staff on #886 (heal, dash and attack), but in Play a wizard holds only the two starter staffs (`Flow.START_HANDS`; Play's start takes no loadout along). The owner's other choice, heal and dash, did worse, so the mender keeps its attack until the owner chooses. Its brain heals the most hurt teammate in reach along a clear line through the AI's existing `heal ally` behaviour; the bot feeds that behaviour its teammates' health from a team board of its own. A heal heals whatever it hits, so the bot holds it while a foe is within 40 px of its line out to the heal's whole reach, within 32 px of the mender, or the aim is unknown. The smoke runner, the sweep and the playtest stock Play's bag with the table's cards and build by `staff_cards_requested`, as the builder does. The 4-bot smoke test still wins its pinned seed 11.

Sweep 1-60 (first fight) on current main: heal and attack lost 2; heal and dash lost 11. Before this change it lost 1, on main before #885 and #887. Playtest of the whole crypt biome, seeds 1-5 and 11, fights cleared: 1 bot 6 to 10; 2 bots 10 to 13 (9 with heal and dash); 4 bots 27 to 33. Downs in the 4-bot runs, P1/P2/P3/P4: before 24/22/20/24, heal and attack 11/26/14/12, heal and dash 17/31/19/19. No run reaches the boss without `--sturdy`. The mender gave 27-1071 health a run, and 0 to foes. Tests: `tests/bots/test_bot_builds.gd`.

## 2026-11-20: Equal split: two players of a half each have their own quarter

The owner decided after #880 that in Equal mode two players sharing a half (two pairs, the pair of four players in three groups, or a pair of three players) open their menus each in their own quarter of it, and answered on #881 that the panel goes with its menus (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Equal split: a quarter each for two players' menus"). Feel's equal panes now move the inner seat of a pair whose panels would share one quarter to the half's other quarter, panel and menus together (P2's to the bottom left and P3's to the top right with two pairs), never onto the panel of a seat played on another machine. The HUD slides them by whole pixels as before (a slanted slide no longer rounds to a step longer than 16 px), and a corner panel hides only while its own open menu covers it. Dynamic is unchanged and nothing in the simulation changed. Tests: `tests/hud/test_shared_half_menus.gd` (new), `test_split_equal.gd`, `test_seat_corners.gd`; screenshots `shot_shared_half_menus.gd`.

## 2026-11-20: Equal split: close players share a pane, halves always left and right, the void quarter

The owner answered the equal split's two questions (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Equal split: grouped panes, left and right, the void quarter"). In Equal mode, players standing close now share a pane as the Dynamic split's groups do (same join and leave reach, so no flicker), and the screen takes the equal layout for the groups: one view, the left and right halves, or the quarters. Two panes are always left and right, P1 and P3 too; each seat's corner panel, builder, inventory and reward menu move into their own pane (a new `hud_corners_changed` event; the HUD slides the panel by whole pixels). With three panes the empty quarter now shows the void with its drifting motes through one more kept viewport looking past the biome. Dynamic is unchanged and nothing in the simulation changed. ## 2026-11-20: The d-pad never walks; quick-use bindings

The owner decided that the D-pad never walks and that each D-pad direction can be bound to a usable item (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "The d-pad never walks; quick-use bindings"). Only the left stick and the move keys walk now; the d-pad's arms are four new actions (1-4 on the keyboard). On the held Y map the d-pad moves the teleport pick (the stick no longer does, so the wizard walks on) and a legend under the frame says "Move teleport target" and "Teleport". In the inventory's potions ring a d-pad arm binds the potion pointed at; in play the arm drinks one (a command, `InputFrame.VERSION` 12, replay `FORMAT` 19). Bindings are inventory state, in the player loadout, kept on rejoin; with none left they stay, greyed. No arm is bound at first, and a usable item picked up that no arm holds fills the first free one (up, right, down, left). ## 2026-11-20: The tools' tests in 75 s, not 300

CI's `ci-tests` job (5 minutes) spent about 295 s in `python3 -m unittest discover -s tools`, and #876's run was cancelled at the limit; the owner chose faster tests over a longer limit. One test was 254 s of it: `test_every_rooms_ground_is_up_to_date` draws all 55 rooms' grounds again and compares them with the committed files. It still does, every room, every file. The ground generator got faster without changing a pixel: a corner of `noise2`'s cells is worked out once (`_corner`, a bounded `lru_cache`), a material's nine slabs round a cell (their jittered middles and tones) once a cell instead of once a pixel (`Material._slabs_round`), a pixel deep inside a frame skips the wander, pins and keep-clear circles that cannot change it, and a keep-clear circle is only measured when its square holds the pixel (exact: a length is never shorter than either side). All 55 rooms' art is byte for byte as before, so only each scene's `ground_inputs` hash changed (the generator is part of it); the GDScript and native drawing are unchanged, as their output is. The test also draws the rooms in parallel, one process a core. On this machine (4 cores): the whole suite 307 s to 75 s, the one test 254 s to about 30 s.

## 2026-11-20: No room rerolls; a teleport from the Y map; joining in the start room; 12 to 16 rooms

Six asks of the owner (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "No room rerolls, a teleport from the map, joining in the start room, more rooms"). The room reroll is gone entirely (its item, start count, HUD count, X at a doorway, command, the generator's reroll and the one-room relay of #869 and #870, the signs' turning over), the reward reroll and the shop's reroll sign kept; `InputFrame.VERSION` 11, replay `FORMAT` 18. With the Y map held and no fight round them, a player picks a cleared room with a flick and A and their wizard alone teleports there, materializing, a column of light narrowing where it left. Holding the map while moving no longer opens and closes it in a loop (its frame kept its size only while the view's anchor stood still). Once anyone has entered a room past the start room, a new or returning player waits for the run's end, their panel saying why. A crypt biome has 12 to 16 rooms, four new fight rooms (arena_13 to arena_16), and exactly one shop, checked over a seed sweep.

## 2026-11-20: Equal split screens

The owner asked for equal-sized split screens as an alternative to the Voronoi split (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Equal split screens"). The pause menu's Settings, Interface tab now has "Split screen: Dynamic / Equal", saved with the other settings and sent to Feel; it is this machine's view only, never the simulation's. Equal keeps the Voronoi split's one picture while everyone fits and its smooth split and merge, but once split each player has a pane of their own on their corner panel's side: two players the halves, three or four the quarters, the fourth quarter black with three (it grows in from its corner and is gone before the panes merge). Each pane draws its wizard at its middle and frames its close foes inside itself. The panes are cut by the same bisector code the Voronoi split uses (the panes' middles as its sites, the black quarter as one more site no view owns), so the HUD, the mouse, the item hints and the map need no change. Tests: `test_split_equal.gd` and additions to the split screen, pause menu, Feel and events tests; screenshots: `shot_split_equal.gd`.

## 2026-11-19: Enemies aim worse early; the starter staffs spread

The owner found the enemies too good at aiming (they led the player) and asked for it to be tunable, starting sloppier, and for the starter staffs to spread a little. Every profile already had a lead (0: at the target, 1: where the shot meets it) and an accuracy (a seeded aim error, up to 14° × (1 − accuracy) a cast); an enemy's are now tuned by one balance file, `content/enemies/profiles/aim_tuning.tres` (`AimTuning`, core/base). It follows the room's depth level (the run's scaling by depth alone, the room's scale with one player: kept in the biome map, `BiomeMap.room_levels`, told by Flow as `run_room_leveled` and kept by Difficulty on each enemy as `Actor.room_level`; the owner decided depth only, so more players never sharpen it): in a biome's first room an enemy leads by 0.3 of its profile's lead and errs 3 times its error, sharpening in a line to its profile's own numbers at level 1.5 (the crypt's deepest room solo); an elite is half the rest of the way sharper. Two global knobs, `lead_scale` and `error_scale` (1), multiply every enemy's lead and error. A shooter in the first room now leads 0.15 and errs up to ±6.3° (was 0.5 and ±2.1°). Test bots are not tuned. The four starter staffs (curl, star, moon, crystal) have 3° of `spread`, the staffs' existing seeded spread. Tests: `tests/gameplay/ai/test_ai_aim.gd`, `tests/core/test_staff_spread.gd`, `tests/gameplay/difficulty/test_difficulty.gd`.

Four view changes the owner asked for (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "The view: close foes, the black void, dark rooms, big signs"), all in Feel. The party camera and each Voronoi view now frame the enemies close to their wizards (same room or bridge, and sealed in a fight or within 384 px) as well as the wizards, zoomed out smoothly no further than 0.5, the wizards always kept in and the split's cells still holding them. The void between a biome's slabs is pure black with a sparse drift of motes given off only outside the rooms and bridges, within the motes' limits. A room nobody has entered is covered wholly in that black and opens by shrinking 16 px blocks over four frames. Every doorway's kind sign is three times bigger (21 px), lit by a light spot of its kind's light and kept clear of the room's dark; it rises in by rows as a wizard comes within 320 px and sinks back once none is near. Tests for each; screenshots in `tests/screenshots/shot_view_framing.gd`.

Five asks of the owner (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Reward items, pick 1 of 3, a shop's reroll sign"). A cleared room's reward no longer pops up: each present player's offer lies as a small chest in their colours in the room, and their A at it opens the pick; an absent player's still waits (rule 4), and a chest left unopened is lost with its biome. ## 2026-11-19: A room reroll may change the kind, and lays only that room

Two follow-ups to the rerolls the owner decided on 2026-11-19 (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Room rerolls: another kind, one room laid"). A room reroll may now turn the room into any kind of the same door and break a kind's count, as long as the biome still has every fixed kind and fights enough on every way to the boss (`rule_problems`'s new `capped` false); the new kind is picked by its weight over one more than the rooms of it the biome holds, then a placement of it evenly (`BiomeGenerator.kind_weight`). In the crypt this still changes kinds rarely, since each kind but the fight has one template and a template stands once a biome (an open question). The reroll now lays only that room again (`BiomeWorld.relay_room`: its layout, its grid cells in a whole lay's order, its place), and Feel's views read only that piece again (the room hearing's `relaid`; the dark, tints, air, mouth looks and signs, the Y map, the spectacle's walls and mist), so no other room's mist, cover or look starts over: 76 to 82 ms against 670 to 716 ms for the whole crypt biome in the test. A restore of the rerolled world, and a fresh lay of its map, give the same grid and hash. Tests: `test_room_relay.gd` (new), `tests/gameplay/run/test_room_reroll.gd`.

## 2026-11-19: Room rerolls and reward rerolls

The owner asked for two new currencies, a room reroll and a reward reroll (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Rerolls"). Both are stacked items in each player's currency bag (content/rerolls), personal and droppable, and every player starts a run with one of each (the run stats `start_room_rerolls` and `start_reward_rerolls`). X in the reward picker spends a reward reroll and rolls that player's offer again from its own table; X at an unvisited room's doorway, where its sign shows, spends a room reroll and the Run area rerolls the room (`BiomeGenerator.reroll_room`: a new kind, template or mirroring that fits the room's bridges and keeps every rule of the map, filled again), which Flow lays in place. The signs, the open Y map, the reward menu's cards and the corner panel's two new counts all change Smooth. Two new commands (`InputFrame.VERSION` 10, replay format 17).

## 2026-11-19: Coins are walked over; a dying foe drops every coin

Two small changes the owner decided on 2026-11-19 (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Coins walked over"). A coin a player dropped is now taken by walking over it, like the floor coins, not with A: the first seat's driven body whose feet come within 10 px takes it into its own bag (never shared), but its dropper only once its feet have left it (`PickupRule.WALK_REACH`, `walked_over`, `GroundItem.waits_for`); keys and other items keep A, and a coin is never the A item in reach. A dying foe now drops the coins it took later (a player's, taken while possessed) as plain coins where it fell (`Inventory.drop_taken`, from the Rewards area's `enemy_died`), every time and also for an add or a boss, instead of losing them; the coins it came with keep the shared rule, and its keys still go with it. Tests: `tests/gameplay/items/test_items.gd` (a dropped coin walked over by one player only, its dropper only after stepping off, a key still needing A; a possessed foe that took coins dying and dropping them as plain coins, none made or lost).

## 2026-11-19: A reward room says what it holds

The owner asked that the reward rooms state which card type they contain (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "A reward room's type"). Each reward room's type is now decided as the biome is made, a pure function of the seed (the generator's step 7, its own key, so no other roll shifts): an item kind from its table's lines by weight (a card, a potion, coins), for a card one of the card kinds its cards are, and for an infusion one of the elements (`RewardType`, kept in `BiomeMap` as hashed state, `RoomKind.typed_rewards` on the reward kind). Each wizard still rolls their own choices there, but among items of the type, widened a level at a time where it has none left: a fire room offers Fire and another infusion, a modifier room two modifiers, a potion room two potions, a coin room its coins and then anything of the table (`RewardRoll`). The owner then asked for more potions so a potion room has real choices: three new ones, all on today's two effects (a small healing potion, 15; a large one, 60; a vigor potion, 5 more most health and 20 healed), in the reward room's table and the shop's shelf. The type travels with `room_rewards_due`. The view tells it: the reward room's bridge mouth sign shows small glyphs beside its kind symbol (item kind, card kind in its HUD colour, element in its palette light), rising with it by rows, and the held Y map shows them under the room's symbol once it draws it (`reward_glyphs.gd`). Other room kinds are unchanged. Tests: test_biome_generator.gd, test_rewards.gd, test_special_rooms.gd, test_party_map_view.gd, test_events.gd.

## 2026-11-19: Keys are each body's own, in a currency bag; a rejoin keeps its mana

Two changes the owner decided on 2026-11-19 (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Keys in the currency bag and a rejoin's mana"). The coins bag is now the currency bag (`currency`, `SessionRules.bag_currency`), holding the coin stack and a new stacked item, the key (`KeyBase`, content/keys; `ItemKinds.KEY`; a bag of two kinds tells them apart by id, `ItemKinds.kind_in(bag, id)`). A key lying on the floor goes into the bag of the first standing body a seat drives that reaches it (a possessed foe's too), and walking into a shut key door spends one of that body's own keys and opens the room for everyone; with none the door stays shut and the seat's hint says "Needs a key" (`key_needed_shown`). Flow's `LockDoors` no longer counts the party's keys (`party_keys_shown` is gone); each corner panel's key count is its shown body's, read from its bag. A rejoining wizard gets each staff's mana back as it left (`LeftPlayers` keeps it by staff number; `Inventory.staff_mana`, `set_staff_mana`). No command was added, so `InputFrame.VERSION` is unchanged; the replay format is 16, since a recorded loadout names the renamed bag. Tests: `test_special_rooms.gd`, `test_rejoin.gd`, `test_key_count.gd`, `test_shop_hint.gd`, `test_item_kinds.gd`, `test_content_determinism.gd` (a key taken and spent, twice and through a snapshot).

## 2026-11-19: The keys on the HUD, a rejoin's inventory, and three small rules

Five changes the owner decided on 2026-11-19 (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "The keys on the HUD and a rejoin's inventory"). Every corner panel now shows the keys the party holds beside its coins, a small brass key and the count, played in and out by whole pixels (`hud/widgets/key_count.gd`, told by `party_keys_shown` from Flow's LockDoors). A player who leaves and joins again mid-run now gets back its whole inventory (cards, staffs, potions, coins), not only its coins: Flow keeps the leaving wizard's player loadout (`LeftPlayers`) and makes the new wizard from it, once. Three more small ones: a rolled challenge room now takes its slot before the treasure rooms (the biome generator grants the chance rooms' slots to the lines with the fewest first), a boss always pays its coins whatever its drop chance, and a secret room found by `secret_sense` rests on the Y map in a faint void light of its own. Tests: test_rejoin.gd, test_key_count.gd, and additions to test_special_rooms.gd, test_rewards.gd, test_biome_generator.gd and test_party_map_view.gd.

## 2026-11-19: Enemy coins and a rejoin's coins

Two changes the owner decided on 2026-11-19 (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Enemy coins and a rejoin's coins"). An enemy now carries its coins in its own inventory from the start (`EnemyBase.coins`, with `coins_most` for a range rolled by the run's chance) and drops them as it dies by its `coin_drop_chance` (rolled by the Rewards area, seeded and counted), possessed or not; the defaults (1 coin, chance 1.0; the dummies 0) keep CB10's economy, adds carry none, and the boss's still go to every player. A player who leaves and joins again in the same run comes back with the coins they left with (only the coins: the rest of the inventory still goes with the old body). Tests in `tests/gameplay/rewards/test_rewards.gd`.

## 2026-11-19: Rewards are items

The owner decided that every reward is an inventory item (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Rewards are items"). A reward table's line now names an item by its kind and id; a pick puts it into the inventory, and the reward card shows the item's own icon, name and description (`ItemLooks`), so the reward kinds, their descriptions and the card's kind glyphs are gone. Heals became potions (`content/potions`: a healing potion and a max health potion), kept in the inventory and drunk with A in its Potions bag through a new command (`bag_use_requested`, `InputFrame.VERSION` 9, replay `FORMAT` 15); the max health potion does nothing until drunk, and a healing potion is not wasted on a whole wizard. The shop sells potions in place of heals. Coins became a stacked item in a new coins bag (`content/coins`): the wallet and `wallet_changed` are gone, the corner panel counts the coins bag, a shop's buy takes the coins off the buyer's stack, and a coin picked up still adds one to every player's stack (shared income; an open question for the owner). ## 2026-11-19: Connected biomes follow-ups, second part: the key, the tint's edge, crawling bots

Three more follow-ups the owner decided on 2026-11-19 (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Follow-ups done, second part"). A crypt biome holds up to 3 treasure rooms (at least one in about 9 in 10; two new treasure templates) and 1 to as many keys, generic and shared: any key opens any treasure door and is spent, so the party chooses; each key drops in a room picked by the run's chance among those reached without passing a locked door, never the boss's (`LockDoors.open_rooms`, Flow's `KEY_COUNT_PURPOSE`, `KEY_PURPOSE`). Every room of a biome now draws its own mist where it stands. A downed bot crawls toward the nearest standing teammate, round walls and aside from the enemies ahead, and stops beside it. ## 2026-11-19: The reward menu's cards

The owner asked for a new reward picker (2026-11-19): an offer's choices now show as cards in a row, picked with left and right and A as before (hud/menus/reward). Each card has the reward's icon (a card's own; coins, a heal and more health have a small glyph of their kind), its name and what it does: a card's builder line, or the reward kind's own line (`RewardEntry.DESCRIPTIONS`, so a new kind brings its text; the Rewards area's choice view now carries `description`). The row is laid out by `reward_layout.gd` in the room the menu has (the seat's quarter of the view while several play, the whole view for one): cards 60 to 96 px wide, side by side; four fit a quarter, and past what fits the row scrolls with an arrow each side, keeping the focused card in view. ## 2026-11-19: Connected biomes follow-ups: the shop's A, the mist's place, secret sense

Three follow-ups to the connected biomes plan the owner decided on 2026-11-19 (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Follow-ups done"). **The shop buys with A:** the Rewards area tells each seat the shelf item nearest its standing wizard's feet (`shelf_near_changed`), the HUD shows the item hint over it ("Buy Heal 30 (6)"), and A sends the new command `shelf_buy_requested` (`InputFrame.VERSION` 8, the replay `FORMAT` 14), so the first seat to ask in a tick takes it, from its own wallet; a refused buy (too few coins, no room, a heal while whole) shakes the hint (`shelf_refused`). Walking over an item buys nothing now, so the shelf sells heals (a new line, 30 health for 6 coins). A ground item in reach still comes first, then the shop's item, then the altar and a waiting reward: what the wizard stands at decides what A does. **The mist lies at its room's place:** the rect, its mask and its parting begin where the room's layout stands, the mask of the walls and holes as mirrored there (`PieceShape.laid_walls`), as #851 did for the art. **`secret_sense` shows secret rooms on the Y map:** Rewards tells each seat's run stats by name (`run_stats_told`), and the map view draws, on that player's map only, each secret room within `secret_sense` pixels of a room the party entered, worked out each frame from the party's map, so it saves nothing and is the same on every machine. Also: docs/LOG.md's leftover merge markers from CB13's merge are gone. Tests: `test_rewards.gd`, `test_shop_hint.gd`, `test_mist.gd`, `test_party_map_view.gd`, `test_events.gd`, `test_panels.gd`; `test_content_determinism.gd`'s shop now buys with A.

## 2026-11-19: The 4-bot smoke test plays one seed

`tests/smoke/test_four_bot_smoke.gd` failed now and then in full parallel runs ("player 3 is down at the end") and passed on reruns. The cause was a random run seed, not timing: the runner started its run with Play, so each start took a random seed (`SimSeed.fresh`), and about one seed in eleven makes a fight the bots lose (6 of the first 66 seeds: a fighter downed and never revived, since the bots do not revive, or a stalemate past the time limit). Each seed's run is the same to the game second on every start, loaded or not, so it is neither timing nor nondeterminism. None of the six lost runs logged an error. The runner now records its run (`Frames.record`) with the replay seed `REPLAY_SEED` (11: no fighter downed), which deals the run's seed, so every start plays the same run; `-- --replay-seed=N` plays another (`--replay-seed=3` is a lost one), and the result line prints both seeds. Reviewed by a fresh-context agent; changed after it: the argument read from both argument lists (a web page's too), replay seed and run seed named apart, freeing the runner stops it. Regression test: `tests/smoke/test_four_bot_seed.gd`.

## 2026-11-18: Connected biomes CB6: the Voronoi camera

The connected biomes plan's sixth step (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "Built (CB6)"): the split screen for real, for 1 to 4 local seats, view only. One picture (the party camera) while this machine's wizards fit at its widest zoom; past that a view a group (wizards within 160 px share one), up to four, each looking where its group is laid on the screen as the party stands so it leans toward the others, the screen cut along the bisectors (`split_camera.gd`, `voronoi_cells.gd` moved in from the spike). A split starts as the picture drawn and a merge ends on it: every view eases, and only the divider changes at once, 3 px a pixel a frame, growing in and shrinking out (Smooth). Online each machine splits for its own seats only. Drawn as CB1 settled (`split_screen.gd`): a SubViewport a view sharing the world, shown by a rect whose shader cuts its cell and draws the divider; the root's cull mask leaves the world to the views and keeps the HUD, so the corner panels stay in the corners. The light is right in every view: a buffer a view, and the apply rect, lit-only motes and mist find the view they are drawn in by their canvas matrix (`light_views.gdshaderinc`), one material for all. What reads the picture by the screen reads the views published on the root (`ScreenViews`, core/display): the mouse's aim, each player's item hint in their own view, a body's looks on screen, the mote sources. The edge markers are in screen pixels, only for a teammate in no view. CB1's fallbacks are a ladder a frame-time budget climbs while split and over the owner's targets (16.7 ms desktop, 33 ms web, four views; `split_budget.gd`): a cheaper light a view, then half and a quarter of the motes. Tests: 1 to 4 seats in a twelve-room biome's rooms, the cells covering the screen, a split and a merge with no jump, the drawing, the light's buffers and the hints.

## 2026-11-18: Connected biomes CB5: the biome generator

The connected biomes plan's fifth step (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "CB5 done"): `BiomeGenerator` (gameplay/run) makes a biome's map as one pure function of the run's definition, the content, the templates' shapes and the seed. It rolls the room count and a map style (`MapStyle`, data: long, branching, loop, hub and spokes as a few weights), the kinds (each line's fixed `least`, its `chance` rooms, the rest fights by weight), the way to the boss (fights enough on it, the boss a dead end at its end, so deepest), the rooms off it, and loops where two free sockets line up a bridge's length apart and the rules still hold; every room in any of its four mirrorings, a template once a biome, on the 32 px grid; then each room's scale (by depth to `scale_depth`, and players) and enemies. `MapRules` has the biome numbers, `KindRule` `least`, `chance` and `dead_end`, `RunRuleChange` targets for them, and `biome_problems` names a run whose content makes no map. On made-up content of every kind (until the crypt has the templates) every style makes sound maps over hundreds of seeds; a map of the crypt's own three socketed rooms is generated and laid. The crypt's run carries its biome numbers but is named not makeable today (no boss room with a socket); the row run stays the default until CB8.

## 2026-11-18: Connected biomes CB3: room life

The third step of the connected biomes plan (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "CB3 done"): a biome is played. `Flow.start_biome` lays a biome's map (`BiomeWorld`) and gives each room its fight (`RoomFight`); the rooms' state is Flow's (`RoomLife`: entered, started, sealed, cleared; each place awake or asleep). A fight starts as a wizard's own body steps onto its room and **seals it one way** (a body in a sealed room is stopped at its mouths, `Actor.passes_mouths`; a teammate outside walks in), and is cleared with its last enemy. **Which room a body is in** is its own state (`Actor.room`, the grid's places, `RoomGrid.place_at`). **A place sleeps** while nothing acts in it (no wizard, enemy, decoy or shot): its bodies are skipped by the Tick and Combat (`Actors.asleep`), so it waits as if no time passed; a shot wakes it. Teleports cross an open mouth, never a seal; a swap across a seal fizzles and goes across a doorway only along a walkable way (`CarrierSwap.may_trade`); a portal carries nothing out of a sealed fight; a possessed enemy leaves its room (cleared without it) and fights for the room it ends in. A downed wizard crawls in a biome. The room events carry the room's number (`Events.FIRST_ROOM`: a new world); every piece is announced (`room_built`), so a mirrored piece's ground is drawn mirrored, and Cover plays the start room's cover only. The projectile manager's target grid is DERIVED, linked again after a restore. Tests: `tests/gameplay/flow/test_room_life.gd`, and a split party in two sealed fights played twice and through a snapshot in `test_content_determinism.gd`. The row run stays the default.

## 2026-11-18: Connected biomes CB1: the camera spike

The connected biomes plan's first step (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, CB1): the stress fight drawn as a Voronoi split of 2, 3 and 4 views, a SubViewport a view sharing the root's world, each shown through a polygon the shape of its cell, the root drawing only the cells and the HUD, and a light buffer a view; kept as a stress test, `tests/benchmark/split_views.gd` (also `--split-views` in the Linux Test and Web Test exports). Measured in this container on software renderers only (llvmpipe under xvfb; the Web Test export in headless Chrome on SwiftShader), so only the ratios count: the split is drawing only (the tick does not move), a view through a SubViewport costs what the root's own drawing does, and each view draws the frame again (4 views about 2.9 times no split in the browser). The fallbacks, measured at 4 views: the light off saves 22 to 26%, the fewest motes 6 to 7% on llvmpipe and 20% in the browser; every view also draws every shot (the batches are never culled). The numbers and the order to try the fallbacks are in the plan; the owner sets the targets from them, best from a GPU run. For CB6: a view's light is drawn where the root's camera puts it (one apply rect, one buffer), fixed by a buffer and apply rect a view on visibility layers of their own.

The run found a bug in the projectile manager: a body freed after the last step (an enemy killed in the frame a run ended) was still in the target table when the next run began, its numbers renewed short of the table, and every later step indexed past them. `begin_run` now drops freed rows first, and drops any live one it reports too. Regression test in `test_projectiles.gd`. Reviewed by a fresh-context agent; changed after it: a warning when no GPU time is measured (WebGL has none), the extra light buffers follow the views just before the draw, the measure flags turned off on exit, tests for the views' cameras, their freeing and the settings put back, and the plan's notes on what the spike does not measure (what each view frames, cover, the root's world-following layers).

## 2026-11-18: Connected biomes CB2: the biome world

The second step of the connected biomes plan (docs/milestones/connected-biomes.md, "CB2 done"): templates get **sockets** (`RoomSocket`, core/base; drawn in the editor by `SocketPreview`, tools/), and a template is a `PieceShape` to a biome, its 32 px rule, its sockets on the lip and every socket reaching every other checked in all four mirrorings. The crypt's first **bridge** (`content/rooms/crypt/bridge/crypt_bridge`, a walkway drawn from its shape). The **mouth cell** (`RoomGrid.MOUTH_CELL`): shots stop there (the native hit pass reads it as a wall unchanged), a wizard's walk goes through and a free enemy's stops (`WallSlide`'s mask, `BodyKernel` the same to the bit), sight sees through it. The **biome map** (`BiomeMap`, gameplay/run) is small hashed state; `BiomeWorld` (gameplay/flow) lays it, its pieces and the grid over the whole biome. The **grid's room layers are DERIVED**, laid again from the room or the map; a 12-room biome costs the hash its map (about 1.1 KB) where its layers would cost about 310 KB; the projectile manager's target grid still grows with a biome's area (about 190 KB for 6000 × 4000 px, named for CB3). The antechamber, the nave and the ossuary court got sockets and are joined in a hand-made test map, walked through by a wizard in `tests/gameplay/flow/test_biome_world.gd`. The row run stays the default.

## 2026-11-18: The Old Broom's Sweep strikes what is seen, and a faster flick (owner's playtest)

The owner: "The old broom boss' sweep attack is very hard to read. It feels unfair, since it scoops players up way outside its visual appearance (it seems). It can fire faster bullets more often also." Found: a dash's pass was grown by its body's spell scale on top of the body's own size (the Sweep struck 62 px round the broom's middle, the Big Sweep 100 px, its bristles reaching 44), and a dash had no telegraph lane at all. Now (boss plan decisions 37 and 38, the owner's picks): a pass is its mover's hurt shape times its cards' growth only (`CompiledSpell.card_scale`; 41.6 px for the broom), the Big Sweep has one `bigger`, a move's lane is as long as its way and as wide as its pass (`Carrier.pass_radius`, `pass_length`; every dashing enemy's), and the dust flick is `triple · bolt · bolt · bolt` on a 1.2 s cooldown. Tests: `test_move_pass.gd` (a spell scale widens no pass), `test_old_broom.gd` (no Sweep wider than its bristles; both Sweeps' lanes as wide and as long as they strike). Reviewed by a fresh agent: it found the first lane had no length (a dash "reaches" nowhere) and its test passed for nothing; both fixed. Later the same day, on the owner's word (decision 39): a move's lane lies on the floor under the bodies and has no margin, a pass strikes only its front half, and every telegraph lane is subtler (docs/STYLE.md). Side effects for the owner to judge: the Big Sweep is 1.3x weaker and about 1.18x as fast (2.8 s, was 3.3 s). The full suite passes but `test_net_rtc.gd`, which cannot open a UDP socket in the session's container.
