## 2026-11-18: Sound AU2: the sound tool

The sound plan's tool (docs/milestones/sound.md, AU2): `tools/sound.py bake` turns a recipe (`<id>.sound.json`, in a folder of its own) into its variants' `.ogg`, an `AudioStreamRandomizer` `.tres` with its family's jitter (or one looping `.ogg` for a held sound) and the folder's `assets.json`, its level placed on its family's target from docs/STYLE.md and its true peak checked; `sheet` writes a contact sheet to hear a PR's sounds by; `check` (and its test) finds a stale or missing sound without reading audio. The synth (`sound_synth.py`: seven sources, envelopes, swept filters, drive, crush, a room) and the meter (`sound_meter.py`, BS.1770) are standard-library Python; only baking needs ffmpeg. The owner's three style samples are its golden recipes (a whoosh, a fire hit, a glass ping), and fingerprints of a reference recipe a source and shaper guard the synth's sound. No sound is committed yet. The plan's list of ambience files with no recipe is seven, not three (the accents too).

## 2026-11-17: The broom's hall: its light and its strokes again

The owner, on the broom's hall: "You need to rework the lighting. And the center circle has some spiraling arms going out from the center." The swept ring's strokes are now faint broken arcs going round its middle, not spokes spiralling out of it (`tools/ground_materials.py`, generator VERSION 5, every room's ground scene drawn again, only the hall's art and outline changed: its candles moved, and the slab is kept clear round them); the hall is lit as the other crypt rooms are, the shared shaft of light on its middle in place of the wide one (gone, with its art), the dust a shade darker than the swept stone so the ring reads as the lit middle, and the candles moved in off the slab's edge. Then the owner: "Remove the center light column. It is not pretty": the hall has no shaft now, its candles its only light. Tests: a fixture test that strokes go round, not out; the hall's test asks for no shaft.

## 2026-11-17: The crypt rework is done

The crypt rework (docs/milestones/crypt-rework.md, the owner's "Redesign the crypt", approved 2026-11-16) is done, every step built and merged one session at a time: CR-S took out the sandbox (its showcase staffs went to the hub's rack); CR2 built the generator's pieces (pinned and cleared frames, wall art and faces, pits and their cracks, slabs, grit toward the edge, strokes, the crypt's edge styles and ground materials); CR-G the crypt gate and the crumbling crypt pillar, with `crypt_edge` for the owner's floating rooms; CR3 to CR7 drew the five rooms again as floating slabs, each with its landmark and its light: the antechamber's fallen column under the shaft, the nave's sarcophagus ringed by candles and its first pit, the ossuary court's walls of bones, the crossing's ring round a fallen-in floor, and the broom's swept ring. Along the way: the light shaft and the wide shaft, the candles, the rule-1 AI test playing every room's spawns, and the template tests reading shaped rooms. No crypt room is tiled now; the old tiles stay as the tests' and the style guide's references.

Left for the owner: `.github/workflows/platforms.yml` (owner-only) still passes `SCENES: sandbox,wave`; it should be `hub,room`. The crypt pillar's longer-lasting cover in the broom's hall is written into the boss plan for OB17's playtests.

## 2026-11-17: Crypt rework CR7: the broom's hall

The last reworked crypt room (docs/milestones/crypt-rework.md, CR7). **The broom's hall** (boss_broom) is a floating slab drawn from its shape (`boss_broom/boss_broom.shape.json`, `crypt_edge`), with its own dark behind it, no pit (the boss never falls) and no gate (cleared, the run is won); its four pillars are crypt pillars, its dust heaps where they stood. Its landmark is the swept ring: dust over the whole hall, then a ring of clean flagstones with the strokes of a broom round its middle, the dust lying in drifts round it and heaped paler against the slab's edge, the rubble, skulls and heaps out in the drifts, its own cover fields keeping the cobwebs out of the ring. Its light is a wide shaft on its middle (`content/rooms/decor/beam_wide.tscn`, new, shared) and ten candle clusters where the ten wall torches hung. The `dust` and `swept` materials were tuned (quieter dust, clean stone in the ring); `RoomScan.floating_room_problems` takes a room's number of gates; the boss-fit check keeps a boss clear of holes too. The crypt pillar's 60 health (against the cracked pillar's 32) lets the hall's cover last about twice as long; written into the boss plan for OB17's playtests. Its test, `content/bosses/old_broom/test_old_broom_hall.gd`, lives in the boss's folder (the hall is the boss's, decision 28, which now also lists its ground art). The rule-1 AI test passes from every spawn; the fight shots looked at.

Reviewed by a fresh-context agent; changed after it: the hall's test moved from a kind-based test in `tests/` into the boss's folder (no general rule tuned to the broom), the deletion list given the hall's ground art and manifest lines, the ring checked inside the dust at all four corners, ten candles and no torch checked by scene, the docs' sizes and the dust's wording corrected.

## 2026-11-16: Boss intro BI2, the game's part

The boss intro's simulation (docs/milestones/boss-intro.md, BI2). **The data:** `BossIntro` (core/base, new) on `EnemyBase.boss_intro`: a portrait sheet, at most three lines, an accent ramp, a dark and a reveal cue, the timeline's moments as constants, and `problems()`; the story check holds every boss's lines to the speech rule (`StoryValidator.check_speech`). **The rule:** `SessionRules.boss_intros` (on by default, off for automation), the replay format 10. **The hold:** `BossIntroHold` (gameplay/flow, new) stops the game at the end of a run's room's setup for the first boss with an intro among the room's own enemies, by both ways into a room; the command `boss_intro_next_requested(player, page)` (a new last kind, the frame's version still 4) turns a page only while it is still the page, and the last lets the game go; it stacks with the pause menu and a waited-for pad, closes when its boss leaves, the last seat leaves or the room's enemies go, and is restored by a snapshot, told open again, not fresh. `boss_intro_opened`, `boss_intro_paged` and `boss_intro_closed` are new; `boss_shown` says whether the boss has an intro. Presses made during the intro are dropped as it closes (`Frames.drop_presses`), so a staff pressed then casts nothing as the fight starts. Reviewed by a fresh-context agent; changed after it: a staff button held through the close (a keyboard's Space is A and staff 1) stays out of the frames until let go (`Sim.held_through`, state), and a restore with no intro open tells the HUD closed (regression tests for both). A step through its own stop now runs while the Tick pauses the tree for it (`SimArea.is_stepped`): no area used one before, and it never ran.

Tests: `tests/gameplay/flow/test_boss_intro_hold.gd` and `tests/content/bosses/test_boss_intros.gd` (new), `test_tick.gd` (the step through its own stop under the pause), `test_lore.gd` (speech), `test_session_rules.gd`, `test_events.gd`, `test_boss_presence.gd`, `test_boss_bar.gd`. ## 2026-11-16: Hub rework HR-H2, falling, and everything that sees holes

Bodies fall into holes (hub rework HR-H2, docs/milestones/hub-rework.md, "Holes" 4 to 6, questions 8 to 10), in any room; the hub's cracks and the void round its island are holes now (the generator's new `void` setting; the hub has no wall left, its art unchanged). **The fall:** a body that walks and ends a move over a hole with nothing crossing falls for 0.6 s (`HoleRules`), down to everything that asks but not downed; then a wizard is back on its last ground (where it last stood clear of every hole), protected, a fifth of its most health lighter (nothing in the hub, `RoomBase.falls_free`), and anything else dies of it through Combat, the kill of whoever last hit or pulled it, so a room can be cleared by sweeping strays into a crack; a boss is put on the nearest ground instead; a body downed over a hole falls and comes back still down; outside the room is a hole too, so a long dash off the edge falls. `body_fell` and `body_returned` tell it; it is all state, and Feel draws it from that (the body shrinking and darkening into the void, motes off it), so a joining machine draws a fall in progress. **Everything else:** the AI walks round holes and shoots across them, a crack is no cover, its own dodges and dashes never end over one; no grass over a hole, no floor mark, an item let go over one lands on the ground, an add's ring spot takes the nearest ground, and a spawn, a door, a placement or a footprint over a hole is a room problem.

READMEs: core, gameplay/actor, gameplay/ai, gameplay/feel, gameplay/player, content/rooms. HR3 (the plaza and the paths) is next.

## 2026-11-16: The Spitter, a crypt enemy that sprays

A new stray, **the Spitter** (`content/enemies/spitter/`): a snake staff (an olive snake coiled round a wooden staff, jaws open, one eye; the owner's direction), holding a staff of the new Spray carrier. It keeps to its spray's short reach, telegraphs (0.8 s, its lane now a widening cone), then holds a cone of Blight acid on a player for about a second, turning slowly after them (the new `sprayer` profile), and joins the crypt's enemy pool (cost 3, from row 1). Two engine changes made it play as designed: a held spray pours after one wind-up instead of winding its staff up before every puff (`Carrier.pours_held`), and a telegraph lane widens to a cone's width at its far end (`Spells.carrier_lane`).

## 2026-11-16: Hub rework HR-H1, holes in the grid

Any room may now have holes (hub rework HR-H1, docs/milestones/hub-rework.md, "Holes" 1 to 3), general engine work; no room has any yet (the hub's cracks stay walls until HR-H2, which also brings falling). **The room:** a `StaticBody2D` exactly on `RoomBase.HOLE_LAYER` (2) with rectangles marks them; `RoomPlaces.hole_rects` reads them, the room's size counts them, and Flow hands them to the projectile manager with the walls. **The grid:** the manager keeps them in a hole grid over the wall cells (state, cleared with the walls and the bounds; `set_holes`, `add_hole_body`, `is_hole`, `has_holes`), a wall over a hole winning, and a walking grid of the ground and the holes merged, which `slide_body` hands the native kernel for a walk (the plain ground for a `crossing` move), so `WallSlide`, `BodyKernel` and their test are unchanged; `walk_clear` is ready for the AI's walking (HR-H2). **The move:** in a room with holes a body's step is slid in two parts (`Actor._move_over_holes`): first what crosses (the Mover's `crossing`: its dash and everything done to it, a pull, a swap's own move, a recoil, a carry, Combat's push), then its own walk, which stops at a hole's lip. A teleport, a swap and a portal's exit put a body over a hole at once. Where the plan had a jump mark its tick as crossing, a walk from over a hole crosses instead (it is not pushed back out), which also keeps a body pushed into a crack while it walks away; the plan's "Built (HR-H1)" says why. A room with no holes moves a body exactly as before.

Reviewed by a fresh session; changed after it: a walk while something crosses, its circle already over a lip, is slid against the ground alone (and dropped if it would set the centre over the hole), since a push or pull slower than the body's radius a tick (a Slam's at 60 ticks a second) was undone by the walk every tick and a Plow carry pinned at a lip (regression tests at the game's tick); the velocity is summed in its old order again, so a room with no holes moves to the bit as before; the plan's rules 3 and 4 say what was built.

Tests: `tests/core/test_projectiles_holes.gd` and `tests/gameplay/actor/test_move_holes.gd` (new), `tests/content/rooms/test_room_places.gd`; `test_wall_slide.gd`, the native kernel's, the state and the determinism and snapshot tests unchanged. READMEs: core, gameplay/actor, content/rooms. HR-H2 (falling, and everything that sees holes) is next.

## 2026-11-16: Hub rework HR1 and HR2, the plan and the island

The owner asked for "a complete rework of the hub" that looks "cool and inviting". **HR1:** the plan, `docs/milestones/hub-rework.md`, worked out with the owner over mockups in one session and approved ("Go with your picks!"): the hub as the Landing, a magic island at the bottom of the shaft (a plaza round the stone, the landing at the top under a beam of light, the fire pit lit as a campfire, the staff rack, the training ring, the altar's dais, the junk yard), organic lines everywhere, a coast of light and motes, cracks into the void that are **holes** (a new kind of cell: walked round, dashed, teleported and portaled over, fallen into), and everything **built for every room**. Two fresh-context reviews of the plan against the code (the first: the stone prop's layers, the campfire, a second mote source, the generator writing into an edited scene; the second: holes in narrow cracks pushing bodies out, the AI's line of fire, the hole grid as state) are written into it.

**HR2, the island:** a room's ground can now be drawn from a shape file (`tools/ground_shape.py`, standard library only: the room's ground scene, its walls, its rims and drips as mote sources, and its art in `art/grounds/<room>/`), in an edge style (`content/edges/magic`) and ground materials (`content/grounds/turf`, `earth`), both new content folders with READMEs; a crack's edge fades smoothly with its width. `MoteSource.band_length` runs a line's colours in bands, the seam's. The hub is drawn from `hub.shape.json`: 1600×1088 px, a dark turf island with a glowing banded coast, seven cracks (walls until HR-H2), a rock face hanging into the void, embers rising and drips falling; ambient 1.0; everything placed moved to the island's middle. Tests: `tools/test_ground_shape.py` (new), `test_hub_data.gd`, `test_source_motes.gd`, `room_scan.gd` (walls at any depth); screenshot `hub_island`. READMEs: content/rooms, content/edges (new), content/grounds (new), content/motes, tools. HR-H1 (holes in the grid) is next.

## 2026-11-16: Boss OB16, the boss bar and the entrance banner

A boss now has a bar and an entrance (boss plan OB16). **The body tells it:** a boss body (its data's `boss`) gets a `BossPresence` look, added by the actor as a boss's `Phases` are, so a boss is still data with no scene of its own; it draws nothing and is never stepped. As the body enters play it sends `boss_shown` (its id, its name, its lore line, a mark at each phase's health share), then its health and every change after (`boss_health_changed`: hits, heals, a player joining), and `boss_hidden` once as it dies or its body leaves the tree; all three are new view-only events (the plan had the HUD read the bar's health from events it already heard and its end from `enemy_died`, which the HUD cannot: it hears only the wizards' health, and `enemy_died` carries a node). **The bar** (`hud/panels/boss/boss_bar.gd`): at the top middle for everyone while a boss is in play, its name over a 200 px health bar with a notch at each phase's mark, dim once passed (a shape, never colour alone), between the top corner panels. **The banner** (`boss_banner.gd`): its name and lore line over play in the upper middle, 0.3 s in, 3 s held, 0.6 s out, stopping nothing and under the menus. **The sound:** Feel plays the `boss_intro` cue on the boss's body as it is shown. A new screenshot script, `shot_boss_bar.gd`, records the entrance and the bar in the boss's room. See the plan's "Built (OB16)" for the choices made.

Tests: `tests/gameplay/actor/test_boss_presence.gd` (new: one presence on a boss and none on a stray, shown once with its name or id, lore and marks, its health after hits and a player joining, hidden once as it dies, hidden and shown again as it leaves and comes back, only its health after a snapshot), `tests/hud/test_boss_bar.gd` (new: the bar's name, health and marks from the bus, two bosses, cleared by a run's end and start, its fit between the corner panels; the banner's name and lore, its fade, its place and width, no empty lore line), `tests/gameplay/feel/test_feel_sounds.gd` (a boss enters with `boss_intro`, a stray with nothing).

Reviewed by a fresh session (nothing serious); changed after it: the bar sits under the menus and the loading screen, the banner waits while the game is paused, the presence always shows its boss once the body is ready (a body a snapshot makes again shows its banner but plays no sound, since the actor table does not know it yet; the plan says so), `boss_shown`'s name is `boss_name`, the docs say how the bar is cleared in play (each boss's `boss_hidden`), and the bar's test measures it against the real corner panels and the pad line and checks a passed notch dims. Not done: a test through a real snapshot take and restore (the look holds no state; `rebuild_derived` is what a restore calls).

## 2026-11-16: Boss OB15, held sounds, music with intensity and the sound sheet

The game can now sound every moment of the boss fight, though it has no sound files yet (boss plan OB15, decisions 24 and 25). **Held sounds:** a cue's new `held` part (`CueBase`) is a sound that loops from its caster's body while its moment lasts: a wind-up cue's until the cast leaves, a move's until the move ends, else its seconds; Feel's new `sounds.gd` plays it and every one-shot where it happens in the room (2D voices, heard 1,600 px away, so a telegraph is heard off screen), at most 3 voices of one sound at once. **A staff's own moments:** `StaffBase.cast_cue`, `move_cue` and `wall_cue`, besides its cards' cues (`Spells.staff_of`, read by Feel); a phase look's held `sound` and starting `cue` (`PhaseLook`); `EnemyBase.death_sound` in place of the death cue's sound. **Music:** `MusicTrack` (core/base: sound ids as layers, stacked or one entry at a time, a fade) on `RoomBase.music` and `EnemyBase.music`, which overrides the room's while that enemy lives; its phases raise the intensity (`EnemyPhase.intensity`); Feel's new `music.gd` plays it, every layer in step. All drawn only. **The sheet:** the broom's cues (`broom_scrape` and `broom_big_scrape`, its Sweeps' lanes with a held scrape, the Big Sweep's louder; `broom_swish` held while it sweeps; `broom_wall`, `broom_swat`, `broom_ignite`), its burning and splinter, its music (`boss_broom`, and `boss_broom_fire` over it in phase 2); the pillar's and the dust heap's states' cues; `boss_intro` for OB16's banner.

Tests: `tests/gameplay/feel/test_feel_sounds.gd` (new: where a sound plays, the voices of one sound, held sounds following their body until their carrier, wind-up, seconds, body or phase ends, a staff's wind, swish, wall and cast cues in a world, a death's own sound, a phase's held sound and cue, a body first seen in its phase, the room's music and an enemy's over it, its intensity by phase, stacked and entries, fades), `tests/gameplay/feel/test_feel.gd` (the voices moved to `sounds.gd`), `tests/content/enemies/test_enemy_data.gd` (every cue an enemy names exists), the broom's own (every id of the sheet), the cracked pillar's and the dust heap's. READMEs: core, gameplay/feel, content/cues, content/bosses, audio. OB16 (the boss bar and the entrance banner) is next.

Reviewed by a fresh session; changed after it: a staff's move cue (renamed from `shown_cue`) plays only for a carrier that runs its caster along, so the `on wall` pulse a walled Sweep sets off no longer swishes again for 4 s, and only a move's held parts wait for `carrier_ended` (any other cue's last their seconds); a new track fades each old layer out from its own level (a silent layer no longer comes up as it goes); a one-shot takes a voice that is done before cutting one short; the phases and music watching moved out of `feel.gd` into `body_sounds.gd`, which sees every body afresh in a new room; a death's own sound plays even with a death cue of no weight, and no cue played inside it takes it; a check that every cue an enemy's data names exists; a regression test for each.

## 2026-11-16: Ground cover GC8, the art and placing

The game has ground cover of its own now (ground cover plan GC8). Three kinds, each a content folder (`content/cover/`, README new): the hub's **grass** (it fills the hub's island, which now has the biome `hub`; fire lights it, it burns whoever stands in it, spreads, goes to ash and grows back after 30 s; frost puts it out), the crypt's **moss** (look only: it sways, bends and is trampled) and **cobwebs** (single webs, sparse; they chill whoever walks through them, never into a freeze by themselves, and fire burns them away for good). Their tufts are pixel art drawn from grid files (`tools/pixel_grid.py`) in a new palette material, **Leaf**, a dull olive kept apart from Player 3's green and Arc's lime. Burning cover gives off embers and smoke (a new mote kind, `smoke`): a cover state names its motes (`CoverState.motes`), and Feel gives them off from every cell shown in that state (`gameplay/feel/cover_motes.gd`, new; at most 48 a frame, only near the view). Fields and a `CoverPreview` in six rooms: a meadow round the hub's dummies with its stone, altar, lectern and the ways to the stone kept clear; corners of cobwebs and a patch of moss in each arena; cobwebs in the boss's arena's four corners.

Reviewed by a fresh session; changed after it: walking in and out of cobwebs stacked their chill into a freeze (the cover area dropped a body's timer as it left and asked again at once on its way back), so a body's time to its next status now runs on after it leaves (regression tests in `test_cover.gd` and `test_cobweb.gd`); three corners of webs were moved off enemy spawns, and a test keeps harmful cover off every spawn's and door's cell and the cells round it; a cover state's motes are checked to be mote kinds.

Tests: each kind's own (`content/cover/*/test_*.gd`, through the real cover area: `tests/gameplay/cover/cover_kit.gd`), `tests/gameplay/feel/test_cover_motes.gd` and `tests/content/rooms/test_room_cover.gd` (new), `tests/core/test_cover_layout.gd` (the game's kinds named by the rooms' fields), `tests/core/test_palette.gd`; `tests/screenshots/shot_cover.gd` shows the game's cover in every template and the hub, and a fire in each. READMEs: content/cover (new), content/rooms, content/motes, core, gameplay/cover, gameplay/feel, tests, art/kit; docs/STYLE.md. ## 2026-11-16: Ground cover GC7, the AI steers off it

Enemies, bosses and the test bots now keep out of harmful ground cover (ground cover plan GC7; the game still has no cover of its own before GC8's art, so nothing changes in play yet). The brain asks the floor round it through the bus (`cover_danger_looked`, new), and the cover area fills a `CoverSight` (core/base, new) at once: a small window of the room's cover cells, marked where a body would be given a status (burning grass, later cobwebs). Every step it walks, the brain probes each cell its move crosses for 24 px ahead and, if one harms, turns to the nearest of eight ways that does not, keeping to one side until it is round (no pathfinder: steering, beside the walls' detour); ringed by fire, it walks on. Standing in harmful cover, `evade` steps it out to the nearest safe cell, target or none, before it fights. The choices the plan left open are in the plan's "Built (GC7)".

Reviewed by a fresh session (nothing blocking); changed after it: stepping out of the fire now wants more than any fight or heal, a teammate's call and commitment included (2.5, from 1.2), with no random swap then, and walks rather than dashes (a dash overshot the exit); the cover area keeps a count of the cells that harm, so a look after a fire, with only ash left, returns at once; the turn's side is forgotten once nothing harms. Targets and items standing in fire are walked round, never reached: left for GC9.

Tests: `tests/gameplay/ai/test_ai_cover.gd` (new: round a burning strip to its target, never stepping in; straight through grass at rest; out of burning grass with and without a target, a bot too; the look's cells; a corner clipped counts), `tests/core/test_events.gd`. READMEs: core, gameplay, gameplay/ai, gameplay/cover. GC8 (the art and placing) is next.

## 2026-11-16: Boss OB14, the AI's run-up, turns and aim lock, and the Big Sweep

General AI pieces, for every enemy (boss plan OB14). **The aim lock** (decision 26): `AIProfile.aim_lock`, the seconds before a cast leaves that its aim stops following its target, measured against the wind-up its cast has left in Spells (`Caster.wind_left`, so a chill or a freeze moves the lock with it); the AI sends `aim_locked` and Feel's telegraph lane flashes (a beat of 0.2 s, `telegraph_beat`). **Turns:** `AIProfile.turns`, how often after an attack it turns to the foe it aimed at longest ago, its target until its next attack. **Round blocking props** (decision 36): a pillar's ground stands in an AI's line (`AISight.ground_clear`, `Projectiles.ground_clear`) and in its ways round (`detour`, `cover`), and a walk at the target or to a point that would meet one goes round it. **The run-up:** a new behaviour, `run up`: a ready move that runs its caster along and hurts (`CastSense.moves_along`) is cast from where its line is longest, a wall's foot facing the open room (`AISight.run_up_spot`, `run_along`, `Projectiles.clear_run`), walking there first. **A phase's staffs start ready** (`Spells.ready_staff`), and a staff may wait before its first cast as its body enters (`StaffBase.first_wait`; `cooldown` now up to 30 s). **The broom:** its Big Sweep `on wall · reach · reach · bigger · bigger · lumber · plow · dash · pulse` (614 px, a 1.2 s wind-up, every 20 s, 12 s before its first), its fourth staff in both phases, so phase 2 opens with it; phase 1 locks its aim at the wind-up's start, phase 2 for its last 0.25 s; both turn round the party. The choices the plan left open are in the plan's "Built (OB14)".

Reviewed by a fresh session; changed after it: a target standing on its own ground (a body with no Mover, a training dummy) is no longer hidden by it (the ground is checked up to its edge), a walk round a pillar ends only once its line is clear of ground too (it chose a new way every step), the run-up gives up after 6 s and casts from where it stands, a turn comes only as an attack ends (not as a phase's stand ends), and the turn memory is sized from its constant; a regression test for each.

Tests: `tests/gameplay/ai/test_ai_run_up.gd` (a pillar's ground in the line and the walk, an enemy walking round a pillar, a staffed one flanking it, a long dash run up to a wall's foot first, a short dash and a teleport not), `test_ai_aim_lock.gd` (no lock, the last quarter second, a lock at the wind-up's start, a chill moving it, the lane's beat, turns round two players and none without), `test_ai_phases.gd` (a phase's staffs ready, a staff's first wait), the broom's own (the Big Sweep, its locks and turns, phase 2 opening with a run-up and the Big Sweep in an arena-sized room), `test_events.gd`. ## 2026-11-16: Ground cover GC6, what it costs

Ground cover is measured (ground cover plan GC6). A new benchmark, `tests/benchmark/cover_cost.gd`, plays the largest room (the boss's arena, 1,243 cells) under a stand-in grass over every open cell with 100 bodies moving in it: no cover, at rest, all of it burning at once, and a fire's front with a fixed seed. It asked for two changes to the cover area, both in how it reads, not in what it does (the front burns the same as on main, roll for roll): the kinds' moves are now tables worked out with the room (`gameplay/cover/cover_moves.gd`), read directly by the loops that may cover the whole room, so a room all burning costs the area 1.8 ms a tick instead of 9.2; and the bodies are looked at only while something can harm one, so a covered room at rest costs the area 0.01 ms instead of 0.22. A spread no longer rolls for a neighbour its element would leave in its own state (none in content had one). Feel keeps each tuft's roll when it lays the room. No native kernel was needed. The numbers are in docs/DECISIONS.md and the choices in the plan's "Built (GC6)".

Tests: `tests/gameplay/cover/test_cover_moves.gd` (new), `tests/gameplay/cover/test_cover.gd` (no roll for a neighbour left in its own state; cover harmful at rest harms with nothing burning). READMEs: gameplay/cover, gameplay/feel, tests. GC7 (the AI steers off it) is next.

## 2026-11-16: Ground cover GC5, the cover area

Ground cover now plays (ground cover plan GC5; the game still has no cover of its own before GC8's art, so nothing changes in play yet). A new simulation area, `Cover` (`gameplay/cover/cover.gd`, stepped after the bodies), keeps each cell of the room's cover in a state of its kind: fire lights it where it lands, burning spreads to the cover beside it by the run's chance, frost puts it out, burning goes to ash, and ash grows back only where its kind says. Whoever stands in burning cover is given its status the way any status is asked (`status_requested`: Combat applies it, resist and all), at once and every half second, props too, so a dust heap or a barrel standing in it lights by its own trigger. Spells tells where an element touches the floor (`floor_touched`, new): a blast, a pulse's ring, a pool as it lies and on each tick, a shot's or a ray's end; never a shot in flight. The area tells Feel what changed (`cover_changed`, new) and Feel's tufts show it. Its state is reset only as a room starts in play (`room_entered`), so a snapshot taken mid-fire keeps its fire when it is restored, in any room. The choices the plan left open are in the plan's "Built (GC5)".

Tests: `tests/gameplay/cover/test_cover.gd`, `tests/gameplay/cover/test_cover_rooms.gd` (the real game: a fire in the run's second room snapshotted and restored into a game in its first, and Feel draws it), `tests/core/test_floor_touched.gd`, and cover now in the snapshot test's rooms (`test_snapshot_game.gd`) and every content fight (`content_fight.gd`), `test_events.gd`. READMEs: core, core/sim, gameplay, gameplay/cover (new), gameplay/feel. GC6 (the cost benchmark) is next.

## 2026-11-16: Boss OB13, phase adds and health by player count

A boss's phase brings its own enemies, so the fight works in any room (boss plan OB13, decision 17). **The data:** `EnemyAdds` (core/base) on `EnemyPhase.adds`: an enemy id, a burst as the phase starts (`count` and `per_player`), waves while it lasts (`every`, `wave_count`, `wave_per_player`), a cap of those alive (`cap`, `cap_per_player`), each "per player" for every player in play after the first, and where they stand (`at`: a ring round the body, or the room's enemy spawns). **The call:** the body's `Phases` asks for each entry on the bus as the phase starts and for each wave (`adds_requested`, counted by the body's own steps, not while it is possessed); Flow stands them for a body it stood, as the room's own enemies stand (`_stand_enemy`: counted for the players, scaled to the room by `enemy_standing`), under the cap, on a ring slid out against the walls and blocking props; a small helper, `RoomAdds` (gameplay/flow), keeps who called which. **The crumble:** when the caller dies its adds die at Flow's next step through Combat (`crumble_requested`, `Combat.crumble`: no hit, nobody's kill, a death like any other), and the room is cleared after the last. **Health by player count:** `EnemyBase.health_per_player`, the share of its health each player after the first adds, counted from the wizards in play as the body stands (`Actor.players`); a wizard joining or leaving changes its most and its health by the same share (`Actor.count_players`, `Health.rescale`). **The broom:** 0.7 a player, and in phase 2 crypt mites, 4 + 2 a player at once, then 2 + 1 a player every 10 s, at most 6 + 2 a player alive, out of the broom round it. The choices the plan left open (an add named by id, per player after the first, the players in the room now, the ring, the crumble a step later, health and the room's scale multiplying) are written in the plan's "Built (OB13)".

Reviewed by a fresh session; changed after it: Flow's step allocates nothing when no add has lost its caller (`RoomAdds.has_orphans`), an add Combat cannot crumble or one gone with no death told is forgotten so the room still clears (the cap counts only adds still there), each call's ring is turned so a wave does not stand on the burst, a phase's adds entries are held to `Phases.ADDS_MOST` by `EnemyBase.problems`, and an add's ground is read from its scene's state, with no body made; new tests for a snapshot restored between the boss's death and the crumble, the sandbox's most, the adds forgotten with the room, and an add gone with no death.

Tests: `tests/gameplay/flow/test_flow_adds.gd` (a test boss of its own: the burst by 1, 2 and 4 players round it and clear of the walls, at the spawns when asked, waves under the cap of those alive, the adds crumbling as nobody's kill when it dies and the room cleared once after the last, only a body Flow stood calls adds, health following the wizards joining and leaving with its share kept), `tests/gameplay/combat/test_combat_crumble.gd`, `test_ai_phases.gd` (the requests, the waves, none while possessed, the next phase ending them), `test_actor_health.gd` (`rescale`), `test_enemy_data.gd` (the entry's numbers and problems, every phase's adds an enemy of the registry), the broom's own (its mites and health), `test_events.gd`. READMEs: core, gameplay (the event table), gameplay/actor, gameplay/flow, gameplay/combat, gameplay/difficulty, content/bosses. OB14 (the AI's run-up, turns and aim lock; walking round blocking props; the Big Sweep) is next.

## 2026-11-15: Boss OB12, `on wall` for moves

A dash now sets off its `on wall` where a wall stops it (boss plan OB12). **The trait:** a new carrier trait, `MOVES_ALONG` (`CarrierTraits`, answered by the dash: it moves its caster along a way over time; a teleport is over at once), is what a move's speed curve, Plow and now `on wall` need: `TriggerBase.NEEDS`' `on wall` row is `any: FLIES | LINE | MOVES_ALONG`, and Plow's row is `any: FLIES | MOVES_ALONG` (was `MOVES_CASTER` but not `INSTANT_HOMING`, the same carriers). **The wall:** the Mover notes a dash it ends held up head on (OB10) or whose carried body was pinned (OB11), and answers it `walled` (a new last value of `caster_move_finished`); its end point (`CasterMoves.end`) fires its `on wall` payloads, then its `on expire` ones, cast by the body that moved. A glancing wall, a dash ended by its time, a pull and a teleport fire none; a shot a dash moves is not stopped by walls, so its dash has no wall either. `on wall · dash · pulse` is a wall bounce that bursts, and the On Wall card says so. **The broom's dust ring:** its Sweep is `on wall · bigger · lumber · plow · dash · pulse`, phase 2's two `timer · on wall · bigger · lumber · plow · dash · slow · fire · pool · pulse`; both still fit their staffs' mana. No native kernel changed.

Reviewed by a fresh session; changed after it: a wall met on the dash's last, shortened step (or a pin then) now counts, since the Mover ends every move the body walked that step (`Mover._stepped`), with a regression test; the On Wall card's description fits the 60-character limit; the needs-row test knows the new trait; `on wall` firing before `on expire` is tested. The test worlds move bodies as the game does: the card-rules world now moves each body with `Actor.move` (against the walls), and the combo arena's moves stop short of the first wall, a dash stopped so answered `walled`. The choices the plan left open (the trait, how the wall is told, where it fires and who casts it, a moved shot, the ring's numbers) are written in the plan's "Built (OB12)".

Tests: `tests/gameplay/actor/test_move_on_wall.gd` (a dash into a wall head on fires there, round the dasher, once, its last step too, before its `on expire`; none in the open or glancing; a pinned carried body fires the carrier's, against a wall and in the open; works on a dash, wasted on a teleport), `test_actor_mover.gd` (`walled` on each ended dash), the On Wall card's new combo `on_wall_dash_pulse` (with the card, without it, without the wall), the card-rules test and `test_trigger_table.gd` (`on wall` on a dash; wasted on a teleport), the broom's own (its dust ring), `test_events.gd`. READMEs: core, gameplay/actor, content/bosses; docs/CARRIERS.md. OB13 (phase adds and health by player count; the broom's mites) is next.

## 2026-11-16: Ground cover GC4, bodies bend it and trample it

Ground cover now moves as bodies go through it (ground cover plan GC4; Feel's look only, decision 4: nothing in the game changes, and the game still has no cover of its own before GC8's art). Each frame Feel's cover (`gameplay/feel/cover.gd`) takes the actor table's bodies, wizards, enemies and props alike, keeps the 32 nearest the party camera's middle and hands them to its shader, which leans the tufts near each away from it by whole pixels. The same bodies walk a view grid of when each covered cell was last walked through (an image a texel a cell, the shader's `trampled` texture): a walked cell's tufts lie flat while bodies are in it and 0.6 s after, then spring back over 1.5 s, drawing their state's new `trampled_frames` (`CoverState`, core/base) or pressed down when it has none; a state that does not bend, such as ash, is never trampled. A frame allocates nothing: the bodies are handed over only when they moved, in two arrays sized once, and the grid only on a frame that walked a cell. The choices the plan left open are in the plan's "Built (GC4)".

Tests: `tests/gameplay/feel/test_cover_bend.gd` (the nearest bodies handed to every batch, a body walking only the covered cell it stands in, flat and springing back, a new room never walked, the trampled frames in the rows, every tuft reading its own cell's walk, 200 frames of 48 bodies make no object, Feel's step bends it), `tests/core/test_cover_layout.gd` (a trampled frame the sheet lacks). Screenshots: `tests/screenshots/shot_cover_trample.gd`, a strip of a wizard walking through the stand-in grass (now shared with `shot_cover.gd`, `tests/support/stand_in_grass.gd`). READMEs: core, gameplay/feel, tests. GC5 (the cover area: fire, spread and the game's part) is next.

## 2026-11-15: Ground cover GC3, the look and the editor preview

Ground cover is now drawn (ground cover plan GC3; nothing plays yet, and the game has no cover kind of its own before GC8's art). Feel hears each room built (`room_built`) and lays its cover's tufts (`gameplay/feel/cover.gd`, Feel's own script): one batch a cover kind, made by `Batches.multimesh()`, drawn by a new shader (`cover.gdshader`, warmed at start) that sways every tuft from its foot in a slow wind on Feel's clock, its tip by whole pixels. It sits at the floor marks' z and before them in Feel's tree, so the marks lie over the cover and every body over both. A cell's state picks its tufts' frames, sway and bend (`show_state`, ready for the cover area of GC5); a state that draws nothing folds them away. Where the tufts stand is one roll a tuft (`CoverTufts`, core/base), shared with the editor: a `CoverField` draws its rectangle's tufts, and a new `CoverPreview` (tools/) draws the whole room's, from the same `CoverLayout.of`, redrawn when a field, a wall or a spawn moves. A preview is a `RoomPreview` (core/base), which `RoomPlaces` passes over and takes out before play. The choices the plan left open are in the plan's "Built (GC3)".

Tests: `tests/gameplay/feel/test_cover_look.gd` (a batch a kind at the marks' z, the shader warmed, Feel's tufts the layout's and the editor's, a room built heard and laid, a cell's state, the wind's clock, the editor's drawing, the preview never in the room shown), `tests/gameplay/feel/test_marks.gd` (the marks over the cover). ## 2026-11-15: Boss OB11, Plow

A new modifier, Plow (`content/spells/modifiers/plow/`, behaviour `plow`; boss plan OB11): what its carrier hits rides along in front of it instead of being passed. **The carrying** is core/spells' (`Plow`, core/spells/plow.gd, owned by the runtime like `CasterMoves`): Spells tells it of each hit of a carrier with the card, and it keeps each carried body with what moves it (a body a dash moves, a shot a dash moves, or a flying shot). Each step it puts the body where it rides, its middle as far ahead of its carrier's as their two sizes (touching), from where its carrier will be; the body's own Mover reads that velocity and moves it against the walls, as any move, so it cannot walk back into its carrier, only out to the side. **A carry ends** pinned (its carried step held up head on along the carry, `Mover.held_up`: walls, a blocking prop's ground, the Wall card's stones), let go with a shove (the dash's pass ends, the shot ends), slipped off (out aside past the pass, `SpellLimits.PLOW_SLIP`: no shove, no pin) or dropped (the body down or gone, its hit blocked), each told by a new event, `carry_ended`. **Combat** arms the pin at the hit (`HitProfile.pin`, twice the hit, `SpellLimits.PIN_SHARE`, × the caster's power, decision 10) instead of pushing, deals it with the slams when the carry ends pinned, or shoves the body on with the hit's push (÷ its mass); a carried body touches the body that carries it for contact damage (decision 11). The body that carried a pinned body ends its dash there, as if it met the wall itself. **Never carried:** a downed body (decision 21), a still one (a prop), the carrier's own body, one carried already; a barrier that takes the hit carries nothing. **On a flying shot** it is a battering ram: its pierce is raised by `SpellLimits.PLOW_MOST` (8), and it never hits a body it carries again. The card: 0.9x speed, 1.4x mana, 1.2x wind-down. Its `RULES` row: what flies or moves its caster, not what is over at once (a teleport). **The broom's Sweeps** take it (`bigger · lumber · plow · dash`; phase 2's `timer · bigger · lumber · plow · dash · slow · fire · pool` twice, its staff 240 mana to fit its cycle). No native kernel changed: the carrying is GDScript, as the move and the pass are (native/README.md, "What stays GDScript").

The choices the plan left open (where the carry lives, which carriers, where a carried body rides, slipping off, the pin, the shove, contact, the ram's pierce, the card's numbers) are written in the plan's "Built (OB11)".

Tests: `tests/gameplay/actor/test_plow_carry.gd` (a dash carries and lets go with a shove; a plain dash carries nothing; pinned against a wall, the pass and twice the hit, the dash ended; a pin ends the carrier's dash; walking out sideways slips off with no pin; it cannot walk back through its carrier; a prop is never carried; a barrier carries nothing; the carrier's contact reaches what it carries; a plowed bolt carries two bodies at once; the compiled pin and pierce), the card's own (`test_plow.gd`: its numbers, its pin, the ram, where it works) and its two combos (a bolt ramming through imps), the card-rules test's probe (the line scene), `test_events.gd` and the broom's own (its Sweeps plow). READMEs: core, gameplay, gameplay/actor, gameplay/combat, content/bosses; docs/CARRIERS.md. OB12 (`on wall` for moves; the broom's dust ring) is next.

## 2026-11-15: Ground cover GC2, the layout

Where a room's ground cover grows is now worked out from the room (ground cover plan GC2, data only: nothing is drawn or played yet). A cover kind is data (`CoverKind`, core/base; its states `CoverState` and triggers `CoverTrigger`, shaped like a prop's), found by the registry's new folder `cover` and, for the editor where the autoload does not run, by `Registry.scan_folder`. A room's cover is a grid of 16 px cells, `CoverLayout.of(layout, room, kinds)`: first the biome's fill (each kind whose `biomes` names the room's biome, by its density, clump size and gaps from walls, doors, spawns and placements), then the room's fields (`CoverField`, a zone with data, its kind and density; a field of no kind clears), never under a wall or on a door, a spawn or a placement. Its chances come from `CoverLayout.hash4`, the engine's hash of the room, the kind, the cell and the roll, never the run's chance, so the game, the editor and every machine agree. A room's `.tres` may turn the fill off (`cover_fill`). Flow tells every room it lays out (`room_built`), at a run's start, in each room entered and on a restore into another room, before the stand-ins and zones are taken out, so the fields can be read.

`RoomZone.data()` and `RoomPlaces.zone_data` carry a zone's data (a zone with data needs no name); the walls' rectangles are read once (`RoomPlaces.wall_rects`) for the room's size and its cover. The choices the plan left open (when `room_built` is sent, a field deciding its own cells, the clumps' shape) are written in the plan under "Built (GC2)".

Tests: `tests/core/test_cover_layout.gd` (the same cover every time, by the game's and the editor's reading, in every room; nothing on a wall, door, spawn or placement; the gaps; fields over the fill and clearing it; the fill off; no biome; keyed by room and kind; the editor's scan), `tests/content/rooms/test_room_places.gd` (a field read with its data and taken out; every room's cover sound), `tests/gameplay/flow/test_flow_exits.gd` (`room_built` for every room laid out; once on a restore into another room, never on one into the room shown), `tests/core/test_events.gd`. READMEs: core, gameplay, gameplay/flow, content/rooms. GC3 (Feel's look) is next.

## 2026-11-15: Boss OB10, moves

A dash's move now has a shape and a width, and walls stop it (boss plan OB10). **Speed over a move:** a card's `move_start`, `move_end` and `move_ease` (ModifierBase; multiplied into the compiled spell) shape a dash from start × its speed, to about full speed, to end × it (`MoveCurve`, core/spells/move_curve.gd), and the move still goes its whole way in its whole time: each step goes the share of the curve's area over its part of the time, worked out exactly, so the AI's reach and the lanes are unchanged. It applies to a body's dash (the Mover reads it from the dash carrier, as it reads homing) and to a shot a dash moves (`Projectiles.move_shot`'s `curve`). The cards are wasted where nothing moves along a way (`Carrier.moves_along`: a dash, not a teleport). They are not the flight's `speed_*`, which change how far a shot flies: on those, Lumber's lurch would have made a Lumber bolt fly a twentieth as far. **Lumber** lurches off and drags to a stop (0.1, 0, 3). **A pass as wide as its mover:** a dash strikes within its body's hurt shape × `radius_scale` (the cards' `radius_mult` and its spell scale), a moved shot within its radius, never under `PASS_REACH`; a wizard's dash is as before, the broom's Sweep strikes about 62 px round its middle. **Head-on walls:** `Actor.move` tells the Mover when the slide changed a step (`Mover.held_up`); a dash that kept under half the way it asked along its own way (under about 45 degrees from straight in) ends there, answered at the wall, and its pass ends with it (`CarrierDash.on_move_end`); a glancing one slides on. Walls, blocking props' ground and the Wall card's stones alike, since the body slides on one grid; a pull or recoil runs its time. No native kernel changed (the moves and the pass are GDScript only; the wall slide is the same). **The broom's dust flick is aimed** (decision 34): `triple · slow · bolt · slow · bolt · slow · bolt`, a 10 degree spread.

Reviewed by a fresh session (nothing blocking); changed after it: a curve that shapes nothing is screened to none where the dash is cast, so a body moves as an even dash to the bit, as a shot's move does (with a test); a dash's pass ends only for the body that moved; an ease alone counts as no curve; the docs say the factors are of the full speed, which is above the even one; the curve's step test counts down in float32 as the game does. The pass's width follows the plan's formula, which with `bigger` and its spell scale reaches past the broom's body (about 62 px round its middle): written down for OB17's playtests to judge.

The choices the plan left open (the move's own numbers, the curve's shape, Lumber's numbers, the pass's width, how "head on" is measured, the pass ending with its move, the spread) are written in the plan's "Built (OB10)".

Tests: `tests/core/test_move_curve.gd` (the curve's factors, the steps adding up to the whole way at any step, NONE, the cards compiled and wasted, a shot's curved move), `tests/gameplay/actor/test_move_pass.gd` (a big body's pass, the least, `bigger`, a dash stopped at a wall striking no more), `tests/gameplay/actor/test_actor_mover.gd` (head on, 40 and 55 degrees, a prop's ground, a pull not ended, Lumber's cast dash lurching and dragging its whole way, a dash with no curve even), `tests/gameplay/ai/test_ai_poses.gd` (the wall pose; the move now ends there), the Lumber card's and the broom's own (the flick: three bolts within its spread of the aim). READMEs: core, gameplay/actor, content/spells, content/bosses; docs/CARRIERS.md, docs/milestones/M2-rules.md. OB11 (Plow) is next.

## 2026-11-15: Boss OB9, the broom's look

The broom now has a look of its own, recorded for the owner's review (boss plan OB9). **Its own face** (decision 14): a `Face` may wear its own art, a `FaceArt` (core/base/face_art.gd): eye and brow sheets in the shared layout (ten eye columns, a brow a mood, a row a look of the face) at a frame size of its own, with their origins; its `style` still sets its blinks and glances. A phase picks a row (`PhaseLook.face_row`). The broom's eyes are an old staff's, heavy-lidded and drooping, under big bushy brows; in phase 2 the lids slant down to the middle and the brows are singed. `FaceArt.problems()` is checked for every enemy and boss, and a face whose art cannot be drawn wears the shared sheets with a warning. After a snapshot the look puts the face's style, glow and row back too (`Face.unstyle`, then each phase's, in `CreatureLook.sync`; the style and glow were not put back before).

**Drawn frames for the extreme poses:** the bristle head is a strip of five (rest; wind-up, lifted and splayed; wall hit, crushed; swat, fanned; and the rest again), picked by `pose_frames`; the burning head has the same five, its fifth the straw catching fire, shown through the phase change before the full flames. **An impatient tap:** `BodyPart.tap` (with `taps`, `tap_every`) lifts and drops a part a few times quickly while the body stands idle; the broom's head, so the whole broom taps the floor. The art is redrawn on its grids: a straight, knotted handle with a worn grip wrap and the empty socket where its gem was (it was the wizard's first staff), a corded binding for the face, the straw in clumps. Its numbers are tuned against the recording: the handle leans 18° (from 14), the head 4° and swishes 5° 3.5 times a second.

**The preview:** `tests/screenshots/shot_boss_looks.gd` plays every boss of the registry in a room of its biome that hosts bosses, against a protected wizard, and records each pose as a filmstrip and a sheet (idle, wind-up, its move, a wall hit, a cast, hurt, the transition, and in its first phase idle, wind-up and move, then its remnant), each frame sized from the body's art. It names no boss and passes with none.

Reviewed by a fresh session; changed after it: the preview read a boss with no phases as being in its first phase, took a second picture in a tick (skipping one), and was sized and named for the broom; it now reads -1, takes one picture a tick, sizes each frame from the body and names clips by pose. The tap now shows only in the idle pose (the Look table's), `FaceArt.problems()` was added with its check, and the poses test checks the face's drawn row through `sync`. Tests: `test_face.gd` (a face of its own), `test_ai_poses.gd` (the tap; a phase's face row, drawn, and put back by `sync`), `test_enemy_data.gd` (every face's own art can be drawn) and the broom's own (its face, its frames, its tap). READMEs: content/bosses, gameplay/actor, gameplay/ai, core, tests; docs/STYLE.md. OB10 is next; OB17 waits for the owner's review.

## 2026-11-15: Boss OB8, poses and layered bodies

A body's look now shows what it is doing, read from states the game already has (boss plan OB8). `BodyPose` (gameplay/actor/body_pose.gd) is kept by any look and stepped last; `CreatureLook` does it for every layered body. Its pose is the first that holds: TRANSITION (a boss standing through a phase change, its AI's state), WALL (its own move, a dash, went less than half the way it would have: a wall met, once each time it is held up, held 0.4 s), HURT, WINDUP, MOVE (its own move carries it, `Mover.is_dashing`, so a pull done to it is not), CAST (its staff animation: the swat) and IDLE. Its motion is a spring that follows the way the body goes, so a body leans in, swings back past upright as it brakes and settles; winding up it rears back from its aim. A layered body is drawn in parts: the `BodyPart`s (gameplay/actor/body_part.gd, a `Sprite2D`) under its `Sprite`, each a frame of any size, in a tree, moved by its own numbers (`lean`, `lag`, `swish`, `squash`, `sway`, `wall_squash`, `shudder`, `pose_frames` for a drawn frame a pose, `fling`, `carries_face`). Both are in the actor kit so that any look can use them and Feel can read a dead body's parts.

A phase look (`PhaseLook`) now also brings its parts' art by name (`parts`) and motes that follow the body (`motes`, from the part `motes_on`). `MoteSource.put` takes `follow` (metadata `mote_follow`): Feel's source motes then move that source's places with its node each frame. A dead layered body leaves a remnant (gameplay/feel/remnants.gd): each part, copied as drawn, flies apart along the floor and up, bounces, lies 1.6 s under the bodies and fades. Each part also breaks into half its share of motes (all of them together at most the preset's most), from the body at its rest scale, so the killing hit's squash is not kept; the pieces are drawn in the order the parts were. All of it is drawn only. A test plays a layered body and a plain one through the same dash into a wall and a hit and finds them equal to the bit.

The broom is layered. Its placeholder art is cut into a bristle `Head` on the floor (it swishes, trails and squashes), the `Handle` behind it (it leans) and the `Binding`, which carries the face. The parts are kept as grids (`tools/pixel_grid.py`) for OB9. The broom hovers (`CreatureLook.hops` false). In phase 2 its head burns (`head_burning.svg`) and gives off embers that follow it. When it dies, the bound head flies off (fling 2) and the handle falls near. Its own face and the drawn frames for the extreme poses are OB9's, with the sheet.

**Found while testing:** the look is stepped once with no time passing at the start of a hit-stop, and the pose ignored that step, so a hit's pose would only have shown once the stop was over. It now reads the pose on that step too, and the poses test checks it.

Tests: `tests/gameplay/ai/test_ai_poses.gd` (bodies made in the test: the order of the poses; its own move leaning in and swinging back as it stops; a pull is not its own move; a wall met once, squashed and shaking, while the dash grinds on; rearing back winding up with its drawn frame; the hurt pose through the hit-stop and the transition's; the face riding its part; a phase's part art and following motes, and `sync`; a layered body moving as a plain one to the bit), `tests/gameplay/feel/test_remnants.gd`, `tests/gameplay/feel/test_source_motes.gd` (a source following its node), `tests/gameplay/feel/test_light.gd` (a remnant is under the spell light, as bodies are) and the broom's own test (its parts, the burning head, its sparks). READMEs: core, content/bosses (the guide's "Its look"), content/enemies, content/motes, gameplay/actor, gameplay/ai, gameplay/feel.

## 2026-11-15: Boss OB7, actor stats

Every actor has stats now (decision 22), general for every body and tuned only in data. `ActorStats` (core/base/actor_stats.gd) is read-only shared data like a card: `power`, `mass`, `spell_scale` and a share resisted of each status and of a possession (`resist_burn`, `_chill` (a freeze too), `_gust`, `_hex`, `_static`, `_possess`), named by an enemy (`EnemyBase.stats`), a boss's phase (`EnemyPhase.stats`, null keeps the last), a prop (`PropBase.stats`) or the wizard's scene (`content/wizard/base/wizard_stats.tres`, the defaults). The actor kit's `Stats` (gameplay/actor/stats.gd; every actor has one, its scene's or one it adds itself) copies them into the body's own state as it is set up and as a phase names some, so relics and difficulty can later change the copy and never the resource; `resist` is a fixed array in Combat's status order, then possess.

Where they count: **power** is read by Combat at the hit from the caster's body, so compiled spells stay shared and a change counts at once: the hit, the slam it arms and the burn it puts on (never contact, plain hits, reactions or heals). A shot whose caster is gone, or whose actor id is another body's now, hits at 1: Spells keeps, per staff, where its generation ring stood when the caster under that id opened and how many builds it has begun since (`Spells.cast_by_open`). **Mass** divides a shove (a hit's push, a pull). **Spell scale** is built into the body's spells at compile time, as `bigger`'s radius without its damage or speed (`StaffCompiler`, `Carrier.scale_size`: the hit radius and its drawn size, a swing's reach too); the Caster compiles at its body's scale and builds again when a phase changes it (`Caster.rebuild`). No hot loop changed, so the native kernels are untouched. **Resist** makes a status and a possession last (1 - share) as long. **A rule changed:** a phase whose condition is met while the boss is possessed starts when the possession ends, so the possessor never loses its staffs mid-cast. The broom's numbers live in its `.tres`: power 2 (phase 2: 2.5), mass 6, spell scale 1.5, resist chill 0.6, burn 0.3, possess 0.7. The room's scale (`Actor.stat_scale`, RG5) still scales health only.

Tests: `tests/gameplay/actor/test_actor_stats.gd` (the defaults change nothing; data to the body's copy; power at the hit, not on a plain hit; a gone caster's shot and a reused id at 1, a mutation of `cast_by_open` fails it; the burn and the slam; mass on a hit and a pull; resist on a burn, a hex resisted whole, chills and the freeze; a possession; spell scale on a bolt's radius and a swing's reach, and `rebuild`), `tests/gameplay/ai/test_ai_phases.gd` (a phase's stats, the staffs built at their size, null keeps; a phase waits for a possession to end), `tests/core/test_snapshot.gd` (changed stats restored into a fresh world, playing on the same), the broom's own test. READMEs: core, gameplay/actor, gameplay/combat, gameplay/difficulty, content/bosses, content/enemies, content/props.

Reviewed by a fresh session; changed after it: a reaction that adds to a status (lightning or void on a chill freezes) was not resisted, now it is (with a regression test); a pulse's ring and a blast grow with spell scale too (a flight's or a beam's reach stays its cards', decision 16); one resist share for a chill and a freeze; a closed caster's ring mark set again; docs in step. **For the owner:** a phase held back by a possession holds back its transition's protection too, so a possessed boss can be hurt past a phase (short on the broom, possess 0.7); and a static discharge follows the caster's power, being a share of its hit. OB8 is next.
## 2026-11-15: Run generation RG8, the map on screen

The pause menu has a Map page in a run of rooms: the run's map, its rows top to bottom from the first room, a line for each link, each room's kind as a letter ("F Fight  B Boss" under it) and "Room 3 of 6"; the party's room is framed in gold, and the rooms it can no longer reach (behind it, another branch) are dim. View only, Back or B to leave. It is opened from the pause menu only: the gamepad has no free button (Select opens the inventory, Start the pause menu).

The HUD never names gameplay, so the run area tells the map as the party sees it (`run_map_changed(rows, kinds, links, at)`) as the party enters each room, an empty one at every run's start, and again after a snapshot's restore (`Run.rebuild_derived`) so a machine joining in a later room shows the same map. The widget (`hud/widgets/run_map_view.gd`) draws only what that event says, with the kinds' names from the registry (`RoomKind.shown_name`). This finishes the run generation plan; the lectern's known gap (it names depths a shallower map lacks) stays, since the map exists only once the run starts.

A fresh session reviewed it and found nothing blocking. Changed after the review: the map's backing is close to opaque, so dim rooms read over a room's walls. Tests now check that the room kinds' names start with different letters and that every run's biggest map fits the page. The pause menu tests gained the cases where a map arrives or changes while the menu is open. The comments note that the view can only hear the map, not ask for it.

Tests: `tests/hud/test_run_map_view.gd` (the map told, its layout, the rooms in reach, empty, the names, a copy), the kinds' letters apart, every run's biggest map fitting the page), `tests/hud/test_pause_menu.gd` (the Map row and page, Back and B, the map going), `tests/gameplay/run/test_run.gd` (the map told on entering each room, after a restore, empty at a run's start and for a run the registry lacks), `tests/core/test_events.gd`. Screenshots: `tests/screenshots/shot_run_map.gd`. READMEs: hud, gameplay/run.

## 2026-11-15: Run generation RG7, the lectern

The hub has a lectern (`content/props/start_lectern/`, a stone stand with an open book, right of the teleporter stone): a seat's A there opens that seat's menu of the stone's run's depths, "Room 1" up to "The boss", and its pick is where the party's next run from the stone starts. The run is generated as always; the party enters the first room of the picked depth's row (the boss pick always the map's last row), and everything else is the same run, its scale included. This replaces the boss plan's OB2c (decision 33) for the generated run, so testing a deep room or the boss no longer needs playing down to it.

The pick is the command `start_depth_picked(seat, depth)`, kept only from a seat standing at the lectern in the hub, the party's one setting (`HubRules.start_depth`, hashed hub state), told with the depths offered (`start_depth_changed`) and named on the stone line ("Starts in: Room 3"). It reaches the run area as `run_begun`'s new `start_depth` (`RunMap.start_at`). A hub started anew forgets it, since a joining machine starts the hub from the replay's header, which carries no setting.

So that deleting the lectern's folder leaves the hub whole, no scene names it: the hub has a zone named for its role, `start_lectern`, and the hub stands the registry's prop of that role there (`RoomContents.stand_in_role_zones`, a general rule: a zone named for a prop role is that prop's place). With no lectern nothing stands there, nobody is ever at one, a pick is refused and the run starts in its first room.

Reviewed by a fresh session (no blocking bug); changed after it: the role zones are stood by `RoomContents.stand` itself, so a run's later rooms get them too; the stone hands its pick to `start_run` (`start_depth`) instead of Flow guessing the stone's run; a downed seat opens no lectern menu; the menu naming a depth a shallower map has not is written down as a known gap in the milestone.

Tests: `tests/gameplay/flow/test_flow_lectern.gd` (the lectern in its zone, a role zone in any room, the seats at it told, the pick kept, clamped and forgotten, the stone's run entered at each depth, Play's run at its first room, no lectern in the registry), `tests/gameplay/run/test_run.gd` (a run begun at a depth: the same map, that row's first room at its scale; `RunMap.start_at`), `tests/hud/test_lectern_menu.gd` (the menu, the pick, closing, what comes first, the stone line, the words), `tests/content/rooms/test_hub_data.gd` (the lectern's place), the prop's own test. READMEs: gameplay/flow, gameplay/run, hud, content/props, content/rooms.
