The boss's room, `content/rooms/crypt/boss_broom/` ("The broom's crypt"): 26×16 tiles (832×512 px, about 1.7 times arena_01) of the crypt's tiles, walls all round and an open middle; its cover is props, so the fight uses it up: four cracked pillars in a loose diamond round the middle and four dust heaps near the corners. An entry door, no exit: cleared, the run is won. It hosts the room kind `boss` in the crypt, so the crypt run's map now ends in it and the broom stands there from the boss pool (by the run generation plan, which replaced the plan's `RoomBase.enemies` and `follows`); the room names no boss, and its first enemy spawn, top middle, is the boss's, the four in the corners left for the phases' adds (OB13).

Two general checks in `tests/content/rooms/test_template_layout.gd`, for every template found by folder: no spawn starts in a prop, and a room that hosts a boss holds every boss of its biome at its first enemy spawn (its hurt shape and ground at its data's size), clear of the walls and the props; with no boss there is nothing to check, so deleting the broom leaves them passing.

Reviewed by a fresh session (nothing blocking); changed after it: the boss check takes every boss naming the biome whatever its `min_depth` (a boss stands in the last row), finds a body's hurt shape and ground by type as the actor does (the kit's default ground when a scene has none), and a spawn stays clear of a prop's touch area too (a wizard starting in a dust heap's would scatter it); the runs and bosses READMEs updated. **For the owner:** Play's crypt run now ends in the broom's arena, with the untuned v0 broom (900 health, up to 1.5 times at the deepest row and 1.3 times with four players) until OB17; and the arena's floor is 768×448, so the Big Sweep's 610 px fits only across, from a side wall's foot (OB14's run-up).

## 2026-11-15: Boss OB5, the cracked pillar, the dust heap and the `touched` trigger

Two ordinary props for the broom's arena, and for any room (nothing in them names the boss). The **cracked pillar** (`content/props/cracked_pillar/`): a 24×48 stone pillar of 32 health, cover that cracks below half (a crack down its shaft, grit trickling) and falls to rubble that neither blocks nor is hit; data and art only. The **dust heap** (`content/props/dust_heap/`): an invincible mound of dust that blocks; a body walking into it scatters it (dust on the floor that blocks no more, still found by fire), and fire on the heap or the dust, a fire hit or a burn, blows it up (`fire · blast · pulse`, hurting everything near, the next heap too) and leaves a scorch mark.

The heap needed one new prop trigger, `touched` (`PropTrigger.When.TOUCHED`): a body that walks (a Mover, not down) overlapping the prop's `area`. The walking body asks: at the end of its move (`Actor.move`) its circle against the walls is checked against the touch areas the actor table keeps (`Actors.set_touch_area`, `touching`, lowest id first; none: one comparison, no work), and each prop it overlaps is told as its own `Actor.touched`, as hits come as `damaged`. A prop lays its area (round its feet: `PropStateTriggers.area`, from `PropBase.area_rect`) only while its state has a `touched` row, so the walkers ask only the props listening now; closing its id takes it away. It fires every tick the body overlaps, so a prop that should hear it once leaves for a state without the row. `PropBase.problems` names a `touched` row with no area. The areas are state in the table, so hashes and snapshots carry them.

Reviewed by a fresh session (nothing serious); changed after it: a state whose `touched` rows cast and none leaves it is a problem (it would cast every tick); the area grows with the body (`Actor.set_size`), as its footprint; a walker ended by what a touched prop did tells no more props; tests walk into the blocking heap from every side, off the grid and slanting. **For the owner:** the scattered dust stays hittable so fire finds it, so it also catches shots crossing it (an invincible hit, nothing happens); a hurt shape that hears hits without stopping shots would be engine work of its own.

Tests: `tests/core/test_actors.gd` (the boxes, the overlap, closing), `tests/gameplay/actor/test_prop_state_triggers.gd` ("Touched": walked into once, only a listening state lays its area, a body with no Mover or down touches nothing, the area problem, a snapshot), each prop's own test. READMEs: content/props, gameplay/actor, core/actors.

## 2026-11-15: Boss OB4, Lumber

A new modifier card for everyone, `content/spells/modifiers/lumber/`: numbers only, the next carrier 0.85× as fast, three times its reach (`reach_mult`: a flying shot's life, so about 2.5× as far at its speed, any other carrier's `ray_length`) and three times as hard, for 2.75× the mana and 1.3× the wind-down, priced above Overcharge's doubling (complexity 3, the `weight` motes it shares with Heavy and Sticky). A dash with it is a 240 px charge that hits three times as hard; its lurch and drag, a speed curve on a dash's move, wait for OB10. The Old Broom's Sweeps take it in place of Reach, which stood in for it in OB3: `bigger · lumber · dash`, and phase 2's two `timer · bigger · lumber · dash · slow · fire · pool`, whose longer charges (about 1.1 s against 0.6) should lay more burning pools; the double Sweep's staff holds 200 mana for its two dearer casts. A fresh-context review priced it up (it first cost 1.75×, cheaper than Overcharge for more), made its line true for flying shots and took a fire burn out of a combo's margin. Tests: the card's own (`test_lumber.gd`: its numbers and price, on a dash and a bolt, four players at once), its two combos (a fire beam burning an imp 500 px away, a bolt hitting for 15) held with and without it by the combo test, the card-rules test playing it on every carrier, and the broom's test checking both phases' Sweeps lumber and every staff affords its cycle. The combo arena moves no caster, so a Lumber dash's longer hit is checked on its compiled numbers only. OB5 (the cracked pillar and the dust heap) is next.
## 2026-11-15: Run generation RG5, scaling by depth and players

Every room of a run is now harder by one scale, from its depth and the number of players: the run definition's `scaling` (`RunScaling`, core/base), a curve per depth (from the first row to the deepest the rules allow) times one per extra player (from one to four), each a `ScaleCurve` (start, end, easing, as a card's speed curve). The generator stores each room's scale (`RunNode.scale`) and spends its enemy budget at it; the players are the seats joined at the run's start (`run_begun(run_id, players)`), so every machine and replay agrees, and a wizard joining later plays the run as it was made. A new area, `Difficulty` (gameplay/difficulty), hears the scale as the party enters the room (`run_room_scaled`) and sets every enemy and boss Flow is about to stand to it (`enemy_standing`, `Actor.stat_scale` on its data's health), so the hub and the sandbox scale nothing. The crypt: 1.5 times by its deepest row, 1.3 times with four players. Two fresh-context reviews: the first found `difficulty_changed` was planned for the enemies' skill (AI10), so the room's scale no longer goes out on it, and docs out of step; the second found two tests proving less than they said; all fixed. The full suite's 4-bot smoke failed at a first four-player factor of 1.75 (three of its four seats fight); the smoke now plays the run unscaled, and measured here main passes it 20 of 22 times, the branch unscaled 18 of 22. Power, spell size and adds wait for the boss plan's OB7 and OB13. RG6 (meta progression's hook) is next.


## 2026-11-15: Run generation RG3b, more crypt fight rooms

A run's map now has four crypt fight rooms to choose from: arena_01 and three new ones made like it from the same tiles, layout only (content/rooms/crypt): `arena_02`, the nave (24×12 tiles, two colonnades and a sarcophagus in the middle), `arena_03`, the ossuary court (20×14, two low walls above a court and two below, open at the sides) and `arena_04`, the crossing (22×14, four sarcophagi and a ring of pillars). Each has four player spawns, eight enemy spawns, the entry and exit doors and, rebased on RG4, two exits below the top door as the arena has (a template with fewer is never picked for a room that leads on), which the layout test checks for every template a run may pick there. A low wall is a wall shape drawn by a `wall_top` sprite of its size. The tests arena_01 had for its layout, look, pillars and torches now run on every template the registry finds (`tests/content/rooms/test_template_*.gd`, `RoomScan.templates`; a room's extent is its walls' box), with new checks: every floor cell can be reached, no rubble or skull lies over a wall, and arena_01's no-scripts and markers-only checks for every template. A fresh-context review found those two checks missing, decor sprites over a pillar or a wall (the new check found two more), a wrong description of the court and rooms instantiated once per sprite in the tests; all fixed. `test_light` reads every room's light spots by registry, and `shot_rooms` takes each template as the game plays it. With four templates the crypt run's first room varies by seed, so `test_coop`, `test_walls_hold` and the screenshots (the base's `pin_room`, on by default) play arena_01 alone through a pinned run, as `test_flow` does. RG5 (scaling) is next.

## 2026-11-15: Boss OB3, the Old Broom v0

The first boss exists as data, playable in the Sandbox's enemy arena: `content/bosses/old_broom/`, an `EnemyBase` with `boss = true` and no script of its own. Phase 1 ("Tidying up") has its Sweep `reach · bigger · dash` behind a 0.8 s wind-up, a dust flick `scatter · slow · bolt` and a swat `bigger · heavy · swing`; under 55% health it stands 2 s and turns to "Spring cleaning": a burning look, a faster profile and a staff of two Sweeps, each with its own 0.6 s wind-up, that leave slow fire pools behind (`timer · reach · bigger · dash · slow · fire · pool`). Its staffs, profiles and look are sub-resources of its `.tres`, so deleting its folder deletes it (decision 28). It names the crypt as its biome, so the crypt run takes it once OB6 gives the run a boss room. Enemies have a line of lore (`EnemyBase.lore`), held to the story's line rules by `Registry.validate_story` (a boss with none warns); the broom's canon is in docs/STORY.md, and the bosses README is now the guide to making a boss, with the table of what is still engine work. The art is a placeholder (a 96×64 broom and its burning version, a stray's face) until OB8 and OB9. Tests: the broom's own (`test_old_broom.gd`: its data, lore, every staff casting with no card wasted, phase 2), the lore rules, and the enemy data test now walks the bosses and their phases' staffs. OB4 (Lumber) is next.

## 2026-11-15: Run generation RG2, the generator

A run's map is made from its definition, the content there is and the run's seed: `RunGenerator.generate` (gameplay/run) is one pure function to a `RunMap`, rows of `RunNode`s (row, the rules' row, kind, template id, links, enemy ids). The shape: the depth, the first row one room, each row's width within the rules and what the row before can reach, and each room leading to an unbroken run of the next row's rooms, never crossing, every room reached. Then the kinds by weight within their limits, never one that leaves the rest of its row or a later row no line (rows narrower than `width_min` only where the limits allow no more), templates never repeating a parent's while another is left, and the fill: enemies of the biome's pool at the room's depth until `MapRules.room_budget` (16, before RG5's scaling) is spent, or a boss of the pool. A room led to by kinds no one line may follow (`not_after`, which the validator checks by kind, not by link) is tried again with other rolls, then named. A fresh-context review found that a kind limited in the run could take the room a later row needed (about half the seeds of a heavy elite found no map) and that `branches_min` was relaxed for every room where only some needed it; both are fixed, with tests, and the invariant checks no longer repeat the generator's formulas. Tests: 1,000 seeds through every rule on the crypt and five made-up runs, the same seed the same map, every room, enemy and boss deleted in turn, a heavy limited kind, a `not_after` corner, and the map hashed and restored by a snapshot. RG3 (rooms filled by the run) is next.

## 2026-11-15: Boss OB2a, no waves (owner session)

The owner replanned OB2 (decisions 30-33 in boss-old-broom.md): the room selector may use only the real game's mechanics, waves go, and a run is a path of rooms from the teleporter to the boss, the lectern only setting where it starts. OB2a takes out the trial gate's first build and the waves: a room lists its enemies (`RoomBase.enemies`, lines of an enemy or boss id and a count), stood at its enemy spawns; cleared with no room after it, the run is won and ends as a lost one does (from the stone, back to the hub). The wave line, the build break, `wave_started` and `build_break_started` are gone; the `wave_start` cue is `room_start`; the balance probe is `test_room_balance.gd`. The Sandbox's enemy arena lists and spawns bosses (`EnemyBase.FOLDERS`). Paths and exits (OB2b) and the lectern (OB2c) are next.

## 2026-11-14: Items I7, the inventory

Select (Tab) opens a player's inventory, a ring menu on the shared radial widget in their quarter: the four bags with how full each is; the staffs, where a hand's button puts the staff pointed at on that hand (or takes it off) and X drops it; the cards by kind, then counted, X dropping one. A column beside the ring shows the four hands' staffs with their numbers and, while a staff is pointed at, green and red arrows for what it would change. The simulation side is two new commands the player controller hands to the inventory (`staff_hand_requested`, `bag_drop_requested`), a staff keeping its number, cards and mana wherever it goes, and `Caster.empty_hand`. Found on the way: the builder kept an earlier wizard's staffs on its strip back in the hub (now `inventory_announced` lets the HUD start over), and drew a staff's head sheet squeezed into its tab (now one upright frame). Tests: the inventory's hands and drops, the menu with a real wizard, a drop landing apart from the item in reach, the arrows' table.

## 2026-11-13: Pulse rings leave a trail (owner session)

The owner asked whether a Pulse's motes could trail its expanding ring. Feel now lays tail motes (the shots' `tail` kinds, one per ring by its id) over the band the edge swept since the last frame, by area, not by time, so the eased ring's fast start leaves as whole a trail as its slow end, at any frame rate and through a hit-stop; a stopped segment lays none past its stop. The edge keeps its outward sparks, a third fewer (90 to 60 a second per 100 px). Only the look changed: `pulse_rings.gd` remembers each ring's last drawn radius by a new `PulseView.id`; the carrier and the simulation are untouched. Tests: five new in test_pulse_rings.gd; 364 unit runs green.

## 2026-11-10: Netcode spike S2, `net/` over the loopback (owner session)

S2 is the netcode without a network: the new folder `net/` holds the messages and their strict decoder, the host's session (the client's seat is a virtual device whose frames come from the link; the host records and sends every tick, and never waits: a late frame is stood in for by the last without its presses and commands), the client's session (a `LiveFeed` that plays the host's run and sends its seat's frames for the host's tick `t + D`), a loopback link, a conditioner (delay, jitter, loss) and the hash checks, spread over the second so no tick pays for a whole one. Two scripted fights linked this way agree at every tick at round trips of 0 to 200 ms with jitter and 5% loss; the host runs a tick every step through a stalled client and a closed link; a disturbed world is caught by the checks with its areas. The grid's first numbers: a press takes the input delay to act (83 ms on a perfect link, 283 ms at a 200 ms round trip), and loss on a reliable channel is what hurts (1% loss: worst press 320 to 450 ms). The exception to hard rule 4 is drafted for the owner, narrower than asked: only a WebRTC peer and its data channel, only in `net/`. Nothing outside `net/` knows it exists. A second session reviewed it for architecture and coupling: the two sessions now share one base, and the host holds a hostile client to limits it lacked (commands waiting, a tick's frame size, one check a window). 317 unit runs green. Next: S3, the WebRTC extension in the desktop builds.

## 2026-11-10: Netcode spike S1, a live replay (owner session)

The owner gave the go for the netcode spike. S1 makes a replay that grows while it plays, the online client's way to play the host's run, with no network in it: a replay's header and ticks go out and come in as bytes (each tick through the file's strict decoder), `Frames.play_live` plays one, and the Tick asks the frames service how many ticks each physics step runs (none while the next tick has not arrived, up to three to catch up). Whoever plays one is a `LiveFeed` (the seam S2's client session extends): it adds the ticks that arrived, takes the client's own seat's frame every physics step (also while the replay waits) and paces the catch-up. A review in a second session on architecture and coupling moved the sending from each tick to each step and the pacing into the feed, and found that Coop joined seats from device events: seats are now the simulation's (`seat_joined`/`seat_left` from the frames service, a replay's from its frames), which also fixes a pad plugged in during a replay file. A scripted sandbox run played live with jitter, a late start and stalls, its player's seat driven otherwise on the client, agrees with it played whole after every step. A second review round: a frame source never seats, a replay's virtual devices are remembered and given back, a replay ends at the step after its last tick, and the feed holds its own replay and closing. Next: S2, `net/` over a loopback.

## 2026-11-09: Every card, enemy and room through a snapshot: N12 and N10b (owner session)

N12's test plays each of the 71 cards and 9 enemies the registry finds in a five-second scripted fight of its own (`tests/core/content_fight.gd`: two wizards with a scripted player, an enemy holding the same staff, Entities, stepped in the Tick's order), twice and through a snapshot taken at a tick of its own, and a wave in every room on the real game (`test_snapshot_game.gd`). No card parted between two plain runs. Through a snapshot, four did, and N10b fixed them: decoys are made again by Entities (`remake_body`, a hook the snapshot asks of the node a body built in code lives under; a row's decoy is made new each time, and a body built in code is matched by its name, after a second session's review), a shot's card body is VIEW and given again by Spells (its number is the machine's, in the order it met each shader), and `HitProfile` is FROZEN (echo parted on a profile that was an equal copy, not the compiled spell's own). About 7 CPU minutes in fourteen shards, so every run plays all content (DECISIONS). Next: the netcode spike.

## 2026-11-09: Lockstep decided; snapshots N10a (owner session)

The owner asked whether lockstep is right for online play or overkill; the answer (bandwidth of state sync at Spellmerge's shot counts, N1-N9 already built, the cost being input delay over distance) is in DECISIONS, and the owner agreed. N10a then built snapshots: `core/sim/snapshot.gd` saves every state member by the walker's plan and makes a world so again (bodies remade from their scene, references by actor id, `restore_nodes`/`rebuild_derived` hooks). A fresh game restoring a snapshot of a sandbox run, a wave or a horde run and playing the replay on agrees at every tick; the scripted fight, storm, horde wave and a possession restore and roll back too. On the way: the clock is `sim.tick` alone, controllers bind to the body they drive through `bind`/`unbind`, AI behaviours are FROZEN, and a body's nodes added in play are walked. Decoys and the rest of the content go with N12 (N10b).

## 2026-11-08: Feel: one hit look per body and staff a frame (owner session)

`BodyLooks.PER_BODY` is 1 (was 3): measured with 1 per body and 1 per two frames beside it, it took Feel's hit listeners about 7 us a hit lower (storm 17 to 10, crowd 20 to 13; crowd_all 49 to 44 ms a step at 30,000 bolts), the same hits and damage, and the storm's heavily hit bodies show one spray instead of a white blob of stacked ones. The owner picked 1 per body; DECISIONS has the numbers.

## 2026-11-07: Feel: a few hit looks per body a frame, and shake that does not pile up (owner session)

The owner asked whether Feel should bundle per target. Measured: 77-93% of hits land on a body already hit that step. A body now shows the looks of at most three hits of one staff a frame (`gameplay/feel/body_looks.gd`; each staff apart, so a lone cast still shows in a storm), and screen shake raises to a moment's own instead of adding (`Feel._shake`), which the owner found unwatchable in the storm. Feel's hit listeners in the storm from about 36 to 14-16 us a hit, crowd_all 57 to 49 ms a step at 30,000 bolts, the same hits and damage. Tests: test_body_looks.gd, test_feel_repeat_hits.gd (its old "each still sprays" is now three a frame). DECISIONS has the rule.

## 2026-11-07: Shot scale H3, Feel skips a hit's look that shows nothing (owner session)

Past the frame's share of hit flashes and on a hit whose motes do not spray, a hit's cues (its spray, its impact) already drew nothing but still ran their parts to find out. A cue's plan now knows what it can show for such a hit, from each part type's `needs_spray` (`add_cue_part`; the spectacle's `often` motes), and `play_cue` skips it (`_hides_hit`); a part type that says nothing always plays. 76% of a crowd's hit cues are skipped; Feel's two hit listeners about 3.3 us a hit cheaper (crowd_all 62 to 53.5 ms a step at 30,000 bolts), the same hits, damage and drawing. Tests in test_feel_cues.gd; shot-scale.md has the numbers.

## 2026-11-07: Shot scale H3b tried and dropped (owner session)

Built on a branch and measured, never merged: a step's hits as one batch in phases (the retest of a shot whose body died earlier in the batch, so no damage was lost), then Combat's lookups shared across a batch. At 30,000 bolts, with the same hits and damage as one by one, a hit's GDScript went from about 58-63 to 48-49 us: too little for what it asked of every later feature (two paths for hits, phases, a subtle order). The owner dropped it for one event per hit; the code is as before, and DECISIONS (2026-11-07) has the numbers and the rule for later speed work (share work inside a listener's own area, never through a second hit event).

## 2026-11-05: The trail shader is the head shader (owner session)

Since tails are motes, `shot_trail.gdshader` drew only a shot's core and its light; it is `shot_head.gdshader` now, and the names that said "trail" for the head say so: `HEAD_SHADER`, `head_mesh()`, the flags `HEAD_LIVE`, `HEAD_ORBIT` (the shader's `HF_*`, also in `light.gdshader`), the quad kinds `HEAD_Q_LIGHT`, `HEAD_Q_CORE` (renumbered 0, 1), `HEAD_QUAD_VERTICES`, `HEAD_QUAD_KINDS`; `TRAIL_JUMP` is `TAIL_JUMP` (the native kernel's too); spell light's `ROWS_TRAIL` is `ROWS_SHOT`; the benchmark is `tests/benchmark/shot_gpu.gd` (`BENCH: shot gpu`, `--tail-motes`). Gone: the familiars' shader's `trail_share` uniform (nothing set it). Comments and the spells README no longer describe tails the shader drew. Nothing is drawn differently: every frame of `shot_gpu.gd --frames` is pixel-identical to before. Older entries here and in DECISIONS keep the old names.

## 2026-11-05: Shot scale H1 dropped, H2 built (owner session)

H1 (fewer hand-backs) was measured first: a hand-back costs about 3-4 us, 12-15 ms of a 211 ms step at 100,000 shots all tested, under 1 ms in a budgeted step, and an exact run needs the kernel to undo its scan-ahead; the owner dropped it (H3b folds it in). H2: the hit-work budget lost damage (the crowd at the desktop cap dealt 47% of it, the storm at 30,000 shots half). A hit now costs 400 units (measured: one target examined is about 0.13 us, a hit about 50 us), `WORK_PER_STEP` is 150,000 so no shot waits more than one step in a full crowd at the desktop cap, and a deferred shot sweeps its whole untested path when tested, or before it ends. GDScript and native alike; `tests/core/test_hit_budget.gd`; two recorded scenes recorded again. A full crowd at the cap now costs what its hits cost (about 13 ms a manager step on the owner's laptop, 6 before): the caps (H7) and cheaper hits (H3) bound it.

## 2026-11-05: Hits' motes shared by staff (owner session)

A Fireball's impact in a Storm threw nothing: the Storm's hits took the frame's hit rows first. Every hit is now one spray shared by its caster's staff (`gameplay/feel/hit_shares.gd`, planned from the frame before as the tails are): a lone cast's hits spray whole, a crowded staff's are a fair random subset that spray whole (the owner's variant b), and a staff beside a crowd may take 32 of the moments' rows for its first hit. The frame's rows by class are one table at `Motes.FRAME_ROWS`. About +0.2 ms for 300 impacts a frame on desktop; GPU web build not measured. Tests in `test_hit_shares.gd`; DECISIONS has the rules.

## 2026-11-05: Flash kinds (F1 to F4, owner session)

The owner asked whether effects and motes should be one system; no (a flash is one shaped glow, a mote a particle), but flash looks were the one look still code. Built [flash-kinds.md](milestones/flash-kinds.md): F1 renamed Feel's effects to flashes (the cue part key `flash`; the "Effect slots" setting kept, the owner's pick); F2 made every flash a `FlashKind` file in `content/flashes/` (14 kinds), the flash shader reading the shaped kinds' curves from a table and each row carrying its kind and room light in the transform's unused floats (the custom data is half precision in the compatibility renderer), with F3 folded in (shell, fireball and arc are files too; `CUE_FLASHES`, `CueBase.FLASHES`, the light tables and the poof look bit gone, freeing look bit 22); ported exactly (a curve test against the old branches, and a one-off render of 4,752 cases with both shaders: every pixel the same). F4 added a star, rays, a ring of dots and a spiral, with six example kinds on `shot_flash_drawings.gd` for the owner to pick from. Not measured on the web storm yet.

## 2026-11-05: The tails keep their motes' rows (owner session)

The owner still saw Fireballs without a tail in a Storm: the projectile manager gave the tail, but the mote pool's rows a frame were one counter, and the Storm's hits (all before the trails' births) took the tails' rows too, so the births laid last were cut. The pool keeps `tail_rows` (the manager's `tail_births_cap`) from every other write until the spectacle hands them to the births (`open_tail_rows`), natively too; a storm's sheds are back to the rows they had before tails were motes. Regression test in `test_tail_motes.gd`.

## 2026-11-05: Tails and particles shared by staff (owner session)

The owner's Fireball cast through a splitting Storm had no tail: the tails' and particles' budgets thinned every shot alike. They are shared by staff now (`group_of`: the caster's staff from the spell tag; `fair_level` gives a small ask all of it, the crowded staffs share the rest), tails planned before the drawing (native too) and particles rolled per group. Tests in `test_tail_births.gd`; DECISIONS has the rules.

## 2026-11-05: Tails as motes (spell spectacle 2, S5 step 4, owner session)

The owner played "Tails: Off": faster, but not the look, and a held atom stream's motes sat at fixed distances. Fixed the cause (the cards' births were rolled in the first part of each step only) and built tails as motes: with the tails off every flying shot lays its tail by distance (`_lay_tail`, `SPARK_TAIL`, about one every 2 px, random gaps from a phase of its own; native in `ShotKernel.draw_shots` to the bit), `tail_births_cap` a frame with every tail thinned past it, in a tail mote kind (`MoteKind.tail`; `tail`, `tail_streak` the default, `tail_long`) picked by the new "Tail motes" dev switch (`TailMotes`, `tail_motes_changed`). Tests: `test_tail_births.gd`, `test_tail_motes.gd`, the native kernel and motes comparisons with tails; sheet `shot_tail_motes.gd`. Web storm: about +0.3 ms motes CPU, +1.5-2 ms frame against tails off (DECISIONS). The owner plays each pick next.

## 2026-11-05: Every moment a cue: swing, beam and status particles as motes (spell spectacle 2, S6 items 10, 11, owner session)

A swing's sparks and card particles and a beam's splash and card particles are motes: Feel gives them off as births in the trails' own format (`gameplay/feel/births.gd`), thrown after the trails' by the motes' one writer (natively where built), in their spell's cards' kinds. The swing and beam shaders keep only their line, pieces and light (223 to 133 and 360 to 217 lines). A status is shown as its element card: a glow in its tint and the card's motes from a slow emitter; freeze is frost's chill at full depth (whiter, twice the motes); `status_looks` is gone and the status shader is its glow alone. The storm gains `--cards` (another build on its staff); its one web A/B, a 27-beam build: about 0.3-0.7 ms more a frame (wasm scripts up, the renderer's share down). Sheets: swing trails, live beams, statuses, before and after.

## 2026-11-05: Every moment a cue: a flash's sparks are its cue's motes, and a torch is lit once (spell spectacle 2, S6 items 3 and 12, owner session)

The flash shader (`shot_effect.gdshader`) draws a flash's shape and light only: its sparks, a rune's, a cast's, a quake's rubble, a column's motes and the card particles are gone from it (and its dead dash branch), and the effect mesh is six quads instead of 34. The cues that show those flashes throw them as motes parts instead: `spark` from strikes, casts (in the seat's colours, through a new motes key `bare`), runes and falling columns, `glimmer` up rising ones, and their cards' motes where no shed throws them. A torch's painted glow is in the group `painted_light` and hidden while Spell light draws its light spot, shown again with the setting off. One web storm A/B on the real GPU (three pairs): no difference above the noise. New sheet `tests/screenshots/shot_flash_motes.gd`; tests for the sparks, `bare` and the torch.

## 2026-11-04: Every moment a cue: the staff swap follows its body, in a new look (spell spectacle 2, S6 item 9, owner session)

Another staff on show plays the cue `staff_swap` along the whole new staff (a cue now has a line, its way for its size from its point: parts play `along` it, and a motes part with `line` is thrown from all of it; the swap is played at the grip with the staff's length), in the colour and cards of its first spell, following the body: `Feel.play_cue` takes a `follow` node (`CueContext.follow`), whose flashes move with it and end when it goes, and a possession's rings follow their body too. The old bone cloud is the cue effect `poof`; the owner found it not good looking, so the live look is new (quick and sparse: the shaft, from its lower end, `StaffBase.butt`, to its tip, comes apart into a few of the cards' motes with dark gaps between them, a motes part's new `life` key keeping it to about 0.1 s; a ring closing on the head at StaffLook's swap with two sparks; a dim light) with two others to pick from (`staff_swap_b`, `staff_swap_c`) in `tests/screenshots/shot_staff_swap.gd`. Fixed on the way: an effect following a freed body stayed on instead of ending (a freed object read into a typed variable is null). Tests: test_feel_poof.gd (the followed flash against the old poof's code, frame by frame; the grip and the tip on the cue's line; `along` and `line` as part keys; the staff's colour and cards; the end with the body; each look shows at the swap), test_feel_hit_kinds.gd (possession follows), test_motes.gd (a burst with a line), test_staff_look.gd (each staff's butt is where its art ends behind the grip).

## 2026-11-04: Every moment a cue: cast, impact and hit looks (spell spectacle 2, S6 item 2, owner session)

A carrier card's looks are cue files now: `cast_cue` (the flash on the staff and the camera kick, `cast` by default), `shown_cue`, `impact_cue` (every strike of its shots, `impact` by default) and `end_cue` replace the `cast_look` and `impact_look` dictionaries, with eleven new cast cues and `impact`, `impact_ring`; a rune goes off by its `rune_burst` cue, with no branch in Feel. A hit's spray and puff are the cues `hit` and `hit_puff`, and a flash no longer throws sparks of its own: cues name their sparks as motes parts (`often` for one of many a frame, `body` for a body's colour). Cues are read once for playing (`cue_plan.gd`), so the hit path reads no part key; on the web storm the change costs about 0.3 ms of a step's scripts (3%). The sheets match main but for the motes' random throws, the nova cast's draw order, and more sparks on a death in a storm.

## 2026-11-04: Every moment a cue: a shot's end, the death puff, colours as data (spell spectacle 2, S6 items 5, 6, 14, owner session)

A shot whose life runs out plays the cue `expire` (its small flare and a puff of its cards' motes; a motes part with a `share` sheds, one of many a frame, and keeps out of the moments' rows). A death's puff is the last part of `enemy_death` and `boss_death`, in the body's own colour (a flash key `body`); the killing blow's spray no longer turns into it. An enemy's colour when hit is its data (`EnemyBase.hit_color`, ember by default), and the horde gems' look is `gameplay/horde/gem_look.tres`. Three preset fields and two constants went. The deaths', mote sources' and impacts' sheets look the same as before (their motes differ from run to run). Web storm figures in docs/DECISIONS.md.

## 2026-11-04: Every moment a cue: new moments and a light part (spell spectacle 2, S6 items 13, 4, owner session)

Six moments that drew nothing now play cues their areas send (`cue_shown`): a horde level-up (`level_up`, on every standing wizard), gems picked up (`gem_pickup`, on the wizard), a revive (`revive`), a barrier blocking a hit (`shield_block`), a body caught by a magnet (`pull`) and a wave starting (`wave_start`). Each has a starter look from existing parts for the owner to tune (sheet `shot_moment_cues.gd`). A cue can now say how often it may play (`every`, per actor), which keeps a horde's hundreds of gem pickups to a few flashes a second. A cue can also light the room (a `light` part: strength, reach, seconds, colour); a cue with one lights by it alone, its flashes then giving no light of their own. The heal, the level-up and the revive light by theirs (sheet `shot_cue_light.gd`, before and after).

## 2026-11-04: Every moment a cue: reactions, possession, arcs (spell spectacle 2, S6 items 1, 7, 8, owner session)

Three more of Feel's moments are cue files now. Each element reaction has its own cue (`reaction_<id>`: a puff, a ring and a ring of the shot's motes in the reaction's colour, a hold, shake and its sound), and a cue may now carry a colour and a strength of its own (`color`, `power`), applied by one rule; ten reaction fields left the Feel preset. A possession's rings are `possess_start` and `possess_end`. A discharge's arc is `discharge_arc`, and a zap plays its card's own cue `zap` (a spark into the air burns out sooner by a size gate on its part). The reactions' and possession's sheets are the same to the pixel as before; the arcs' sheet plays the cue. Tests in the feel suites; details in docs/DECISIONS.md.

## 2026-11-04: Do shots need tails? A Tails switch, and the dash as cues and motes (spell spectacle 2, S5, owner session)

A "Tails: On/Off" switch in the pause menu's Settings (saved; on by default) turns the shots' tails off: shots are drawn as their cores and lights, no path is sampled or sent, and the motes get the tails' budget (twice today's births in a busy fight). Measured on llvmpipe: the trail batches' GPU time is about a third at 60 shots and under half at 400 and 3,000; the storm's frame 8.3-8.5 -> 7.1 ms. A dash's start and landing are now its card's cues (`dash_start`, `dash_land`: dust, lightning's arc, a ring, its element's flare, its cards' motes and marks), its streaks are a mote kind given off along its path, and its trail is not drawn; Feel keeps no dash state. The owner plays with the switch and picks what the game keeps (S5 step 4).

## 2026-11-04: One particle system (spell spectacle 2, S3, owner session)

Every particle is a mote now. "Particles" has no Off (its least is a Minimal ring of 2,048), so the old stand-ins are gone: the chips effect, the trail shader's card particles (it keeps the tail, lightning's line and a dash's streaks; a dash's cards are motes too, the owner's ask), the hit spray's and puff's sparks (the effects draw only their flash), and an explosion's debris and scorch effects (its surface motes and scorch mark). A dead body breaks into motes of its own art in place of the white-hot copy that fell apart. Sheets: `shot_deaths.gd` (new), the trails, dashes, blasts and mote sources. Details and figures: docs/DECISIONS.md.

## 2026-11-04: Spell light from effects, beams and torches; a room's ambient (spell spectacle P3b, owner session)

Feel's effects (bursts, rings, casts, hits, arcs, blasts) and live beams now light the room round them, drawn from the rows and paths Feel already uploads; the crypt's torches are light spots (a marker with metadata, coloured by the flame's art), and every shot lights, the enemies' too. A room has an `ambient` (the sandbox's is 0.85, the owner's pick); the light's steps are a switch (`bands`: 3 steps by default, 0 smooth), with a sheet for the owner to choose between them. STYLE.md now says the world may take light from spells. Tests in test_light.gd; the light pass measured the same as P3a's on llvmpipe.

## 2026-11-04: Settled motes and the other floor marks (spell spectacle P2b, owner session)

Chips and frost's flecks now leave a one-pixel speck in the floor mark layer where they come to rest, in their own colours, once they lie still (`MoteKind.settles` names the mark; `Marks.settle` keeps them in a wheel of time slots, at most 512 stamped a frame). The motes and marks share one ramp table (`gameplay/feel/ramp_table.gd`). New marks: a burn ring round a nova's caster, a rune's scar where a rune goes off (new cue `rune_burst`, on the rune card), ichor in a dying body's own colours (enemy and boss deaths; a new `surface` colour source), and char, lightning's card mark. Tests: `test_marks` (a speck where and when a mote rests, the caps, the settings, the new cues); screenshot sheet `shot_marks.gd`. Details: docs/DECISIONS.md.

## 2026-11-04: Trail particles as motes (spell spectacle P1d, owner session)

The shots' card particles are Feel's motes now: the trail meshes drop their particle slots (a dash keeps its own), the projectile manager rolls the particles the shader would have given off (the same rates, thinned by the spark pool's share) and hands them out as births, and Feel throws one mote each. On llvmpipe the trails send 42-92 % fewer vertices; GPU time 42 -> 16-26 ms at 400 shots, about 85 -> 72 at 3,000, 22-37 -> 13-21 at 60, for 0.3-0.9 ms more CPU a frame; the storm shows no CPU change. On at every count (`SPARK_MOTES_BELOW`); the web and a real GPU are not measured. The motes look like the motes' kinds, not the shader's particles: sheets `shot_trails` and `shot_trails_shader` side by side for the owner. Tests: `test_projectile_looks`, `test_motes`. Details: docs/DECISIONS.md.

## 2026-11-04: Trails at a high Max shots (owner session)

Reported by the owner on the web build: with Max shots at 10,000 or more, every fire bolt after the sandbox's LB storm had a short tail at a wrong angle. The storm holds every trail row (2 x the cap), and the trail texture was one line per row, 32 texels wide: 20,000 rows made it 32 x 32,768, past the 16,384 a GPU takes. The texture now holds 64 rows a line (2,048 texels wide; 2,048 x 2,048 at the top cap of 50,000), read by `trail_texel` in `shot_trail.gdshader`; `_trail_data` is laid out as before, so the native kernel is unchanged. Regression test: `test_the_trail_texture_fits_a_gpu_at_a_high_cap`.

## 2026-11-04: Floor marks, the layer (spell spectacle P2a, owner session)

Feel has a floor mark layer (`gameplay/feel/marks.gd`): a viewport over the room, one texel a pixel, never cleared until the next room, rendered only on a frame a mark lands. A cue's new `mark` part stamps a mark into it through one batch (`mark.gdshader`); the shapes are worked out from data (`MarkKind`, `content/marks/`: a scorch blot, forking cracks, a frost rime) in the floor's own colours (`RoomBase.floor_colors`, sampled from its art) or the spell's ramp, clipped by the walls. Blast and fireball leave a scorch (frost with a frost card), slam and quake a crack. The rooms' floors moved to z -2 so the marks lie under every body. The pause menu has "Floor marks: On/Off" (`FloorMarks`, `floor_marks_changed`, saved). Tests: `test_marks`, `test_cues`, `test_pause_menu`, `test_events`; screenshot sheet `shot_marks.gd`. Details: docs/DECISIONS.md.

## 2026-11-03: Motes, the pool (spell spectacle P1a, owner session)

Feel has motes: single-pixel particles with no game logic, in a ring of up to 32,768 rows cut into 1,024-row batches (`gameplay/feel/mote.gd`). `mote.gdshader` works out a mote's whole flight from its row and the time since its birth: drag along the floor, a fake height, one bounce, lying still, cooling through its ramp's shades. Kinds are data (`MoteKind`, one file each in `content/motes/`: spark, ember, fleck, wisp, murk, glimmer, chip), their shapes too (pictures of up to 5x5 pixels drawn as text, a frame a picture, over its life, looping or picked, turned with its flight: sparks are streaks, frost glints into a plus), and they come from the spells: a kind names the cards' look layers it is the motes of, and a shot sheds the kinds of its cards' layers in its own colours. A wall sheds chips in colours sampled from the room's wall art (`RoomBase.surface_colors`, leaving out the floor's colour; `debris_colors` overrides). Only the batches written in a frame upload, a batch with nothing alive is hidden, and at most 384 motes are written a frame. The pause menu's "Particles" slider (Off, Low, Medium, High; `MoteCap` in core/settings, saved as `mote_cap`) sets the ring's size through the new `mote_cap_changed`; Off shows the old chips effect. Tests: `test_motes` (the ring, uploads, the frame budget, hidden chunks, the content's kinds, the shader's numbers, kinds from looks, the ramp table, a room's colours, `mote_land`, wall hits, the setting), `test_pause_menu`, `test_events`; screenshot sheet `shot_motes.gd`. Details: docs/DECISIONS.md.

## 2026-11-02: Spells' hit and Feel's impacts (owner session)

Spells' `_on_hit` works out a hit's heading only for a spell with a payload on hit and what a chain carries only while it has jumps left, and skips the siphon, possess, pull and blast checks for a plain hit (`CompiledSpell.plain_hit`, worked out once); `_relay` takes the spell it already has. Feel's `projectiles` is typed as the projectile manager, so its `impact_*` facts are read as members, not by name. Sandbox storm, per call: Spells' hit 11.6 -> 7.8 us, Feel's shot end 3.6 -> 3.2 us; Feel's impact and Spells' shot end about the same. About 0.35 ms a frame in the storm. Bundling the hit events per frame was measured and not done (0.33 us a delivery, about 0.3 ms a storm frame). Tests: `test_staff_compiler` (a plain hit). Details: docs/DECISIONS.md.

## 2026-11-02: The hit's listeners: Feel and the actors (owner session)

Feel's `damage_dealt` (about 14 us a hit in the sandbox storm) found the `Actors` autoload by path on every hit: it keeps it now while it is in the tree (`_actor_table`), 14 -> 13 us. An enemy's own `damaged` listeners (its AI, look and face) cost 1.6 us a hit together, so they were left. The training dummy's damage numbers are written to their labels once a frame, before it is drawn (`DummyLook._write_new_numbers`), not on every hit: a hit 2.9 -> 1.4 us, and with the frame's work about 9 us less a hit. Combat's hit in the storm, the dummy's listener included, about 55 -> 50 us. Tests: `test_feel_repeat_hits` (the kept table follows the autoload), `test_dummies` (the labels are written before the frame is drawn). Details: docs/DECISIONS.md.

## 2026-11-02: A cheaper elemental hit in Combat (owner session)

Where a hit's time goes in the sandbox storm (about 72 us a hit, timed part by part): Combat's own work about 34 us, its listeners the rest (Feel's `damage_dealt` 17 us, the training dummy's `damaged` 10 us, `hit_landed` and `actor_hurt` 5 us). In Combat, an elemental hit walked every row of the reaction table, reading each row's properties: `ReactionTable` now keeps its rows by element and status (`for_element`, rebuilt when its rows change), and a hit puts its status on by its row (`_apply_status_row`, no lookup of the actor), and reads the body's position once. `tests/benchmark/combat_hit.gd` (new): frost on a frozen body and fire on a burning one 20 -> 17 us a hit (-14%); a plain bolt and storm on a hexed body unchanged (10.4 and 19.5 us). Tests: `test_spell_components` (the index follows its rows). Details: docs/DECISIONS.md.

## 2026-11-02: Cheaper trail vertices (owner session)

`shot_trail.gdshader`: the path search (12 texel fetches, most of a vertex's work) runs only for a slot that may still draw (a particle that was given off, a tail pixel while the trail has a tail, a step of lightning's line), and each quad of a trail's mesh is one triangle (`TRAIL_QUAD_VERTICES` 3, cut to its box in the fragment stage), so a quarter fewer vertices. Every frame of `tests/benchmark/trail_gpu.gd` is pixel-identical to before. llvmpipe GPU time, medians of runs taken in turn: 400 shots 76 -> 47 ms (the search 76 -> 51, the triangles 51 -> 47), 60 shots 33 -> 25 ms; 3,000 shots unchanged (about 105 ms: the pool has already thinned those meshes to a few slots). `trail_gpu.gd` takes `--only=` and `--timed=`. Tests: `test_projectile_looks` (the mesh's triangles). Details: docs/DECISIONS.md.

## 2026-11-02: Sandbox storm and a launch's shots made together (owner session)

`tests/benchmark/sandbox_storm.gd` (new): the sandbox's split-and-nova build held among the training dummies, printing shots, hits per dummy and, with `--profile`/`--profile-signals`, where a step goes. Using it: the atom carrier makes a launch's shots together (`ProjectileManager.spawn_batch`, `set_looks`, `Spells._land_shots`), the same result as one by one (`test_spells_land_shots`, every array byte for byte); Spells' step 5.6 -> 2.3 ms and the frame 33 -> 14-17 ms in the storm. Feel skips the flash, hold and sound of a repeat hit (23 -> 15 us a hit; `test_feel_repeat_hits`), and the training dummy hears only its own hits. Details: docs/DECISIONS.md.

## 2026-11-02: Trail meshes by shape (owner session)

Lightning's trails and the dashes draw in batches of their own (`BATCH_*_JAG`, `BATCH_DASH`, `BATCH_ENEMY_DASH`), so a plain shot's trail mesh has no jagged line, no dash streak slots and, with no share for the tails, only its newest pieces' lights: vertices a frame -50% at 400 shots and -81% at 3,000; llvmpipe GPU time 108 -> 70 ms and 403 -> 131 ms. Pixel-identical where no trail changed batch (`tests/benchmark/trail_gpu.gd -- --plain`, new: renders a fixed scene, times it and saves frames). The per-trail work on the CPU and several tail pixels per quad were measured and left (at most 8% and 20%, at low counts). Tests: `test_native_kernel` (every batch compared), `test_projectile_looks` (the shapes, the batches). Details: docs/DECISIONS.md.

## 2026-11-02: Max shots and Show FPS settings (owner session)

The pause menu's Settings page has a "Max shots" slider (`ShotCap.STOPS` in core/settings: 250 to 50,000, starting at the platform's cap) and a "Show FPS" switch. The slider sends the new `projectile_cap_changed`, which the projectile manager applies at once (`set_cap`, so the shots in the air end); the switch shows a frame rate readout at the bottom middle (`hud/widgets/fps_counter.gd`). Both are saved (`projectile_cap`, `show_fps`) and read when the menu is bound. Tests: `test_pause_menu.gd` (slider, switch, save, reload), `test_projectiles.gd` (the event sets the cap), `test_events.gd`.

## 2026-11-02: Projectile caps doubled with the native kernel (owner session)

`DEFAULT_CAP_WEB` 500 -> 1,000 and `DEFAULT_CAP_DESKTOP` 3,000 -> 6,000. The native manager at 6,000 shots steps in 0.3 to 1.3 ms (its GDScript took 2.8 to 5.8 ms at 3,000); what more shots cost now is the hits' GDScript handlers, which hold the web at 1,000 (step scripts about 10.5 -> 13 ms headless). The stress run follows both caps; the horde scenarios keep 500 shots (`HORDE_SHOTS`). The GPU cost of 6,000 trails is not measured. Details: docs/DECISIONS.md.

## 2026-11-02: More shots: the dodge check, trail meshes by the pool, three catch-up steps (owner session)

`Projectiles.threat` shares a per-step grid of shot paths between the AIs' asks (8 asks at 3,000 shots: 11.3 -> 5.4 ms); the trail batches draw a mesh with only the slots the spark pool's shares can fill (particles thinned by a stride, tails cut to a dash's streak slots with no tail share; RTX A3000 at 3,000 shots: trails about 19 -> 6 ms; pixel-identical with the pool full or empty); the engine catches up at most 3 physics steps a frame (was 8). Desktop stress, headless: 343 -> 93 ms a frame, the step 40 -> 26 ms. Tests: `test_projectiles_threat` (grid against the pass, changes within a step), `test_projectile_looks` (meshes and stride), `test_physics_settings`; `test_projectiles_targets` no longer counts the engine's catch-up steps (fingerprint recorded again on main's manager). Details and what was dropped: docs/DECISIONS.md.

## 2026-11-01: The sandbox staff's cog head (owner session)

The `dev` staff has a head of its own (`content/wizard/staffs/dev/head.svg`): an upright eight-tooth cog in the player colour round a gold hub, on a straight shaft with a gold butt, 16 directions by 4 player ramps like the starter sheets; `tip` 18. Sandbox wizards now show it on all four buttons instead of the default curl head. `test_wizard_art.gd` holds it to the same sheet contract.

## 2026-11-01: AIs handle walls (owner session)

`AISight` (gameplay/ai/sight.gd) gives every AI one view of walls (room walls and Wall cards, from the projectile manager's wall grid; `Projectiles.line_clear`). Enemies got a REPOSITION state: they walk round a wall rather than wind up an aimed cast into it. The test bot is a state machine (APPROACH, ATTACK, DETOUR, UNSTICK, DODGE): it walks round any wall in its line of fire and, at a telegraphed shot, heads for cover behind any wall near it (`AISight.cover`), dashing there when its dash is ready (one move step: urgent and ready means dash, so no dodge-only dash); it never asks what its staffs cast (owner). The bots build walls again: the 4-bot run clears with bot 3's `growth · wall` in 7.8 game seconds.

## 2026-11-01: Showcase sandbox staffs, mana per staff, one staff file per setup (owner session)

The sandbox starts with two showcase builds of the trigger tree (`Flow.SANDBOX_START_CARDS`): Y the most shots (`on expire · fire · split ×3 · nova · frost · split ×3 · nova`, holding the 3000-shot cap for about three seconds) and B a decoy turret (`timer · on hurt · decoy`, all 20 slots, none wasted). Mana is the staff's now (owner): `StaffBase.max_mana`/`mana_regen`, one pool per staff in `Caster`, holds and siphon per staff, the panel's bar the staff on show and the builder's the viewed staff (`staff_mana_changed`); enemies' pools moved onto their staffs. Staffs are one file per setup, used as they are (owner): Play and the horde hold the four basic staffs, the sandbox the dev staff (a million mana) on every button; the build channel no longer picks staffs. Bots leave `wall` out of their random builds (it stalled the 4-bot run with 4-slot staffs). Open: each basic staff has the old wizard's 100 mana, so a wizard has four times the mana across its staffs (docs/DECISIONS.md).

The relay clips now show the teammate (a wizard body shots can hit) and the cast direction, so the bolt reaches the imp. Tests in `tests/clips/test_clip_scenarios.gd`.

## 2026-10-29: Card rules R2 and R3 ticked off

R2 and R3 landed on main in #468; ticked off in `docs/NEXT.md`. The rule 2 checks (a card survives a bounce, jump, echo, payload) are deferred to `docs/BACKLOG.md`.

## 2026-10-29: Freezes in co-op (melee I7)

Feel's hit-stop is local now. A hit holds its attacker, its target and the shot that did it (new `Actors.hold` / `spend_hold`, which `Actor.step` obeys, and `Projectiles.freeze_shot`, a frozen shot keeps its life and tests no hits); a kill holds its killer; a reaction holds its caster. Another player, another enemy and every other shot run on. `Engine.time_scale` drops only for `player_downed` and the death of an `EnemyBase.boss`. A body rests `hit_stop_rest` before a plain hit holds it again (per actor). Tests: `test_feel_local_stop.gd` (a hit does not hold another player, a bystander or an unrelated shot; a down still stops the game), `test_projectiles_freeze.gd`; the old feel tests now read the holds.

## 2026-10-29: Screen shake setting (melee I6)

The pause menu's Settings page has a "Screen shake: On/Off" switch. It sends the new `screen_shake_changed(enabled)` event, which feel turns into `Feel.shake_enabled`, and the choice is saved in `user://save.cfg` (`SaveFile.get_setting`/`set_setting`, a `settings` section beside the flags) and read when the menu is bound. Hit-stop, particles and sound stay. Tests in `test_pause_menu.gd` (flip, save, reload), `test_feel_form_looks.gd` and `test_events.gd`.

## 2026-10-29: The Rime Crook and the Bone Scepter (living staffs H5)

Two more strays, content only. The Rime Crook (`content/enemies/rime_crook/`) is a wooden crook with ice fangs and bands that casts frost · hook: it pulls a player toward it from 120 px. The Bone Scepter (`bone_scepter/`) is a skull on a stick whose dark sockets the shared face lights in Void pink; it casts void · rain from 130 px as a zoner. ## 2026-10-29: Staff codes (staffs D2)

A staff copies as one line, `sm1:double,fire,-,bolt` (card ids by slot, `-` for an empty slot, the empty tail left off), and pastes back. `StaffCode` (`core/spells`) is pure text work: it takes only lower-case names, at most 40 cards and 600 characters, checks every id with the registry, and never loads or runs anything from the text. The list builder gained a row of "Copy code" and "Paste code" buttons (system clipboard; the d-pad reaches them between the tabs and the slots); a paste sends one `staff_cards_requested`, so the player still refuses cards it does not own. The radial builder and the pause menu have no code buttons yet.

## 2026-10-29: Bots and the emergent combo report (staffs D1)

The bots (`Bot.build_random`, seeded) and the staff fuzz already existed from A6a/A6b; D1 adds the report. `test_spell_fuzz.gd` now prints `REPORT` lines after each run: the five best of its 600 random staffs by kills and by reactions, and the five costliest by mean step time and by safeguards met (dropped shots, then peak live shots), each with its cards and generated name. The fuzz imps now have 60 health (they had 400 and nothing died). Run it with `ci/build/build.sh --quick --tests` and read `build/logs`; paste a run here when cards land.

Review round: ties in a ranking now go to the lower staff number (`sort_custom` is not stable), each line starts with the ranked value and shows `(unnamed)` for a staff without a name, and a `REPORT WARNING` prints when no staff kills an imp, so an empty report is noticed. Tests: `test_report_ranking`, `test_report_ties_are_total`.

First run (seed 20261026, 600 staffs, 2 s each), the lines that do not depend on the machine (cards shortened):

```
REPORT BEST BY KILLS: 2 kills | Skewering Lazy Patient Beam | staff 44 [on_cast pierce slow sniper sniper beam]
REPORT BEST BY KILLS: 1 kills | Overcharged Echoing Rime Beam | staff 191
REPORT BEST BY KILLS: 1 kills | Stuttering-Magnetic Plump Bolt | staff 315
REPORT BEST BY REACTIONS: 4 reactions | Arcing Bloom | staff 89 [arc nova triple storm lightning faster void]
REPORT BEST BY REACTIONS: 4 reactions | Zippy Bloom | staff 531
REPORT BEST BY REACTIONS: 2 reactions | Bolt | staff 70 [bolt frost fire]
REPORT COSTLIEST BY SAFEGUARDS: 105 | Stuttering-Wall | staff 462 [burst wall mirror split echo]
```

So random staffs mostly do little: kills are rare, reactions rarer (one kind), no staff drops a shot at the pool, and nothing needs a limit yet.

## 2026-10-29: The first cards' looks (staffs C0)

Review round: seen at 4x (`looks_hops_4x`, `looks_wake_4x` in `shot_looks.gd`), hops did not draw at all, for three reasons now fixed: its trail window (0.15 s) cut its particles, the spawn rate was read from the wrong table row (`kind_row` now maps kinds 13 and 14 to rows 16 and 17 everywhere), and its every-other-frame blink hid it whenever a still shot was captured on an even frame (now always shown; the dash lines jump sideways). Wake is brighter dashes. READMEs and comments updated.

The three original modifiers without a look now have one: split a burst (9), chain hops (13, new: a pixel that jumps sideways), faster a wake (14, new: pixels flung back fast); sparks and streaks were taken by lightning, pierce and heavy. Carriers and infusions already had theirs; the numbers were reviewed and kept. `test_projectile_looks.gd` uses echo as its card without a look. Test: `test_original_looks.gd`.

Every cast of a staff now has a generated name: `SpellNamer` reads a compiled cast and builds "Twice-Seeking Firefrost Bolt of Sudden Novas" from the cards' `name_words` (a new field on every card, two words each so a name can vary; a plain bolt is "Bolt"). Same cards, same name (a djb2 hash picks between a card's words). `Spells.staff_view` returns it as `name`, and the list builder prints it above each cast of the plan. Tests: `test_spell_names.gd` (exact names, plurals, growth, determinism, and that every real card has words).

The bolt costs 5 and split costs 1.5 times that, so a volley of three hits gives back about 3 of 7.5: Siphon softens a cast and never pays for it (a first try at 3 per hit made split bolts net positive; a test now keeps a combo's refund under a bolt's cost). All unit tests pass except `test_svg_art` on the Shield icon, already on main (see BACKLOG).

## 2026-10-29: A possessing player cannot strand a room (staffs C)

Found the bug behind the bots' missing `possess`: an enemy that died while possessed sent no `enemy_died` (its team was the wizards'), so flow kept counting it. Fixed in `Combat._die`, added a flow regression test, and put `possess` back in `Bot.build_random`'s bag. All unit tests pass except `test_svg_art` on the Shield icon, already on main (see BACKLOG).

## 2026-10-26: Caster kit (staffs B4)

The wizard now acts on every move a card can ask for. Teleport jumps along the aim and stops short of the first wall; hook and swap pull the wizard to a point over the time asked; recoil pushes it back, with the recoils of a step added up so a mirrored recoil cancels. A later dash, teleport, hook or swap replaces an earlier one, which is still answered (`caster_move_finished`) where the wizard is, so its blast is not lost. Chill and freeze slow and stop walking and dashing, hits push the wizard (combat's knockback), and siphon's refund gives mana back. A move now walks its whole time (before, the last step of a dash was not walked) and is answered by the next step. Tests: `test_player_caster.gd` (14).

## 2026-10-26: Shot look, part 2 (staffs B6b)

Feel's per-form looks are data on the carrier cards now (`FormBase.cast_look`, `impact_look`; the five `.tres` carry the old preset values, the preset has none, the old rune and orbit textures are gone). The shots' particles and bolt trails come from one pool with a global count (`particle_cap`, 1,024): the manager adds up what the drawn shots ask for, particles get their share first, the trails get what is left and thin down to nothing, and only then do the particles thin, every shot alike (the shader reads `particle_share` and `trail_share`). New clips: four players firing the same cards, and a crowd of sparks. Tests: 6 pool tests, the form-look tests read the cards, a clip-scenario test.

## 2026-10-26: Combat kit (staffs B3)

`gameplay/combat` now does what the staff cards need from a fight: every hit has a kind (`hit_landed` before `damage_dealt`), a wizard is hurt by their own spell like any other target (damage, push, status), wizards have burn, chill/freeze, shock, hex and static of their own, barriers (`wizard_shield_requested`, `shield_blocked`), area hits (`area_hit_requested`), several elements on one hit (each reacts with what the one before left), heal hits (`heal_dealt`, which `gameplay/player` now applies), pulls (`pull_requested`) and the heavy wall slam. It sends `kill_landed`, `reaction_landed` and `wizard_hurt` for cards, and `downed_note` for "downed by your own Blast". `gameplay/feel` gives each kind a default stop, shake and sound, the self-hit its own ring, the heal a soft ring, and shows the note. Not switched on yet: `Spells` does not send the shield and pull events or set self-hit on shots (B2 and the cards). Tests: `test_combat_kit.gd` (35), `test_feel_hit_kinds.gd` (11) and four compiler rows.

## 2026-10-26: Trigger kit, part 4 (staffs B2d)

The spell runtime runs the moves, areas and refunds: teleport (a blink along the aim, cut at walls), hook (a hit pulls the caster to it), swap (a hit moves the target to the caster and the caster to the target), recoil (a push back from each firing of the cast, so mirror cancels it); the end of a dash, teleport or hook (`caster_move_finished`) is the carrier's end point, where its blast and `on expire` payload happen; `blast` asks combat for an area hit where a shot or beam ends; siphon refunds mana per hit. Tests: `tests/core/test_spells_moves.gd` (22 tests). **B2 is done.**

## 2026-10-26: Trigger kit, part 3 (staffs B2c)

The spell runtime runs the payload and line carriers: rain (a `form_shown` cue, then its payload on the aim point after its lifetime), familiar and decoy (`entity_spawn_requested`, counted as live until `entity_ended`; a familiar casts its payload on `familiar_cast_requested`; a decoy's end point fires its `on expire` payload and `blast`), wall (a line of still shots across the aim, shields apply) and fissure (a beam through everything on its line). Tests: `tests/core/test_spells_payload_carriers.gd` (28 tests). B2d (moves, areas, refunds) is next.

## 2026-10-26: Trigger kit, part 2 (staffs B2b)

The spell runtime runs the multicast spread: scatter (random directions, seeded per cast), burst (later firings of the group, queued), echo (the carrier again after its strength in seconds, at half damage) and mirror (also fires backwards). Tests: `tests/core/test_spells_multicast.gd` (27 tests). B2c and B2d are next.

## 2026-10-26: Trigger kit, part 1 (staffs B2a)

The spell runtime fires the payloads of the seven new trigger events (on block, on kill, on cast, on wall, on hurt, on reaction, on ally) and runs `relay`. Tests: `tests/core/test_spells_triggers.gd` (10 tests). B2b to B2d (multicast spread, payload carriers, moves and refunds) are next.

## 2026-10-26: Projectile kit (staffs B1)

`core/projectiles` runs the movement and collision behaviours of the staff cards: bounce, ghost, spiral, return, linger, growth, sticky, shield (`shot_blocked`), shots that may hit their caster after leaving the caster's hitbox, shots that pass teammates (`ally_passed`) or hit them (mend), and `expired_on_wall` for `on wall`. The spell runtime hands a shot these from the card strengths, so the cards after B1 (bounce, double and the like) are data. Self-hit and ally-hit are available but not switched on by `Spells` yet: combat has no self-harm until B3. Tests: `tests/core/test_projectiles_kit.gd` (17 tests, including 1,500 shots with behaviours).

## 2026-10-26: The old recipe path removed (owner session, staffs A6c)

Staffs are the one way to build and cast. Removed: the recipe compiler (`Spells.compile`, `builder_view`, the clamps, the budget), the recipe wrappers, the fields only recipes used (`valid`, `reason`, `description`, `complexity`, `depth`, `held` on `CompiledSpell`), the build limits in `SpellLimits` (only the per-step fire and chain caps stay, as engine safeguards) and five events (`build_change_requested`, `spell_swap_requested`, `spell_changed`, `recipe_changed`, `active_spell_changed`). Tests that wrote spells as recipes build staffs through `tests/support/staff_kit.gd`; the limit tests became no-limit tests (a nova with two splits makes 72 shots; four chains jump eight times; payloads nest four deep). Phase A is done, so development is unpaused: the bot starts phase B, and the owner lane opens.

## 2026-10-26: Combos and tests on staffs (owner session, staffs A6b)

The 28 combo files are card rows now (`{"staff": 0, "cards": ["fire", "timer", "orbit", "fire", "split", "bolt"]}`), converted mechanically (infusions and modifiers, the trigger, the carrier, then the payload); all 28 still pass. The clip scenarios cast card rows (a lone card on a bolt; a trigger fires a nova; a multicast pulls three bolts). The fuzz test builds 300 random staffs from every card: no errors, every cycle finite, and the costliest staffs printed (the first run: at most 32 live shots, nothing dropped at the pool, the slowest step 0.3 ms headless).

## 2026-10-26: Player and builder on staffs (owner session, staffs A6a)

The game now plays on staffs. Four buttons cast four staffs (A `cast_a` Space, X `cast_x` left mouse or J, Y `cast_y` right mouse or K, B `cast_b` Q or L); menus activate with A and go back with B; the overlay moved to Start (Back is `leave`). The wizard holds four rows of cards, builds them through `Spells`, offers `free_mana()` (mana minus what `hold` cards keep), and a held button never switches a `hold` off. Dodge is gone: the `dash` card (220 px/s for 0.25 s, no invulnerability) asks the wizard to dash through `caster_move_requested`, and the wizard reports `caster_move_finished`. Flow hands every wizard the `dev` staff (20 slots) in dev and preview builds and the `starter` (4 slots) otherwise, with A `dash` and X `fire bolt`; the staff numbers were retuned so a two-card staff is not slowed by its recharge (starter delay 0, recharge 0.05 s). The builder is new: four staff tabs, the slots and the bag in scrolling lists, picks that fill a staff left to right, the cast plan as text, open at any time, waves included; the quick view and the overlay's Bag drop are gone (owner: "A full builder is always preferred"). Bots build into staff Y and fight with X and Y; the stress runner builds X and Y. The owner's idea of generated spell names became part E1.

## 2026-10-26: The staff runtime (owner session, staffs A5)

The owner paused development (control panel) so the bot would not idle through tasks that wait on A5, and an owner session built it. Staffs cast: four per player, each with its cycle, cursor, delay and recharge; multicast carriers fan out; payloads fire as groups; fallbacks ask combat for an area hit (`area_hit_requested`, which nothing hears until B3); `hold` keeps its shots and reserves mana until the staff is cast again or edited. The tag is now player, staff, generation and node above `1 << 20` (enemy attack tags reach 101,000). Recipes run as staffs of one cast, so the old cast, trigger and combo tests pass unchanged on the new runtime; the three tag tests that assumed two slots now use four staffs. New: `tests/core/test_staff_runtime.gd` (10 tests).

## 2026-10-26: The staff compiler (owner session, staffs A4)

`Spells.compile_staff(cards, staff)` reads a staff into its cycle with the rules of the design (`core/spells/staff_compiler.gd`), and `Spells.staff_view` gives the builder plain values. Every example in the design is a test in `tests/core/test_staff_compiler.gd` (20 tests). Two things the tests taught: wrap-around lets a trailing card reach the first carrier in a later cast (so `fire · bolt · homing` puts homing on a bolt in cast 2, and `double · on hit · bolt · nova · bolt · blast` makes two casts of three spells each), and a 64-slot staff can make more than 128 compiled spells in one cycle, so the tag's node field is 9 bits. Nothing casts a compiled staff yet; that is A5.

## 2026-10-26: Card and staff classes, events (owner session, staffs A3)

The foundation the kits build on, so they never edit the same base file: `MulticastBase` (double, triple, scatter, burst); `FormBase.Motion` DASH to FAMILIAR (`carries_payload()` for rain and familiar), `ModifierBase.Behaviour` SHIELD to RELAY (`behaviour_strength` now up to 256, and every behaviour needs one above 0), `TriggerBase.Event` BLOCK to ALLY, each commented with the kit that runs it; `StaffBase` and the `starter` (4 slots) and `dev` (20 slots) staffs; the staffs events (`staff_change_requested` to `ally_passed`) with their senders; `multicast` as a kind in the Spell atlas and the clip pipeline (`ci/smbot/clips.py`, an owner-path change made in the owner session). Nothing runs the new values yet.

## 2026-10-26: Shared files split (owner session, staffs A2)

So card PRs never edit the same file: the 26 combos are one file each in `content/spells/combos/` (`combos.json` is gone; the test, the clip scenarios and the Spell atlas read the folder), a content folder may list its assets in an `assets.json` beside them (the validator and `test_svg_art` read every one with `art/manifest.json` and `audio/manifest.json`), and no test keeps a card list or count by hand (`test_combos` checks every component the registry finds; the icon count and the drop-list length are derived). AGENTS.md and the spells, art, audio and tests READMEs say so.

## 2026-10-26: Spell visuals round 2, part 1 (#244, p2)

The owner found the shaped shots too big and the combos plain. Bodies are now 0.8 of the old bolt (was 0.62) with three trail marks each (384 at most, still one draw batch). New event `combo_cast`, sent by `Spells.cast` with `Spells.combo_class`: a flat mark on the caster per class, a ring of ticks (boosted), chevrons along the aim (on_hit), a diamond (on_expire), a dashed ring with a ticking hand (timer), ring over diamond (stacked); made in code in `gameplay/feel/combo_marks.gd`, so no assets. Looked at the sheet `combo_marks` and the clips (`combo_fire_fan_orbit` shows the timer ring); a first version that turned the rings by 22.5 degrees came out ragged, so quarter turns only. The resolution question is assessed in `docs/DECISIONS.md` and added to the C2 second-round text. Part 2 (a release burst per multi-component combo) is in NEXT.md. Tests: `test_feel_combo_marks.gd`, `test_spells_cast.gd`, `test_events.gd`, `test_projectile_shapes.gd`; all unit tests pass.

## 2026-10-26: Scrolling drop lists

The quick view's drop list (11 rows) and the overlay's (6 lines of two) are now windows: a longer list scrolls to follow the focused row (`hud/widgets/row_window.gd`), so a twelfth element or modifier no longer pushes the panel off its quarter of the screen. UIFocus is unchanged. The title shows "n/total" when the list scrolls. Tests: `test_quick.gd` (a 16-row list in the quick view and the overlay, the focused row always in view, the window maths). Screenshots of the 11-row lists look as before. Decisions in `docs/DECISIONS.md`.

## 2026-10-26: Lightning, reactions, combos and the report (#243, part 2)

Two reactions for lightning with fire and frost, four rows (a pair each way): **Overload** (`burn` + lightning, `static` + fire: a burst of 4 in 52 px) and **Superconduct** (`chill` + lightning: freeze 1.5 s; `static` + frost: the charge used up for 4 bonus damage). Colours and power are in the Feel preset (`overload` brown, `superconduct` teal, `discharge` cyan; all at least 0.45 apart from the six older ones in `test_feel_reactions.gd`). The discharge now has its colour, the sound id `reaction_discharge` (no `.ogg` exists, so it is silent like the other reaction sounds; listed under Audio needs) and an arc: the event `discharge_arc(player, from, to)` (`core/events`, sent by combat once per enemy zapped) and Feel draws a jagged `Line2D` from the struck enemy to each, from a pool of eight, 0.25 s. Combos: `overload`, `superconduct` (two players) and `shared_charge` (two players, the discharge). Tests: `test_combat_reactions.gd`, `test_combat_statuses.gd` (arc), `test_feel_reactions.gd`, `test_events.gd`, the combo test over the new rows. `content/spells/README.md` now says what a new element needs outside its folder. Clips `combo_overload`, `combo_superconduct`, `combo_shared_charge`. The extensibility report is in the PR; proposals are in `docs/BACKLOG.md`.

## 2026-10-26: Lightning, status and element (#243, part 1)

Added the fifth element, Lightning (complexity 1), and the `static` status. A static enemy hit again by any player's spell discharges to every other static enemy within 96 px for 60% of the hit (at least 1), as plain hits for the same player, announced as `reaction_triggered` `discharge` at each. One step only: no chain, and a reentrancy guard refuses a discharge started inside another. Looks: a new palette ramp "Arc" (acid lime), a forked-arc shot body with a spark trail, an enemy overlay (lime blink, apart from Storm's yellow), and an icon. Sheets `element_shots`, `statuses_4x`; clips `element_lightning`, `combo_static_bolt`, `combo_static_ring`.

Files changed outside `content/spells/elements/lightning/`, and why:
- `gameplay/combat/combat.gd` (+README): the status and the discharge (two packed arrays, a line in each `match`).
- `core/spells/validator.gd`, `core/spells/spells.gd`: `static` as a known status, and its description verb "Charges".
- `core/base/element_base.gd`: `shape` range 0–5.
- `core/projectiles/projectile_manager.gd`, `shot_core.gdshader`: shape 5 (the arc) and its trail mark; the kind numbers moved (marks 6–10, rune 11, orbit 12) because trail marks were "shape + 4".
- `gameplay/feel/presets/feel_preset.gd` (+README): the status look, `status_order`, the discharge's power.
- `hud/builder/builder.gd`, `hud/panels/overlay/overlay.gd`: the drop lists hold every element and modifier, 11 now, which no longer fit a player's quarter at 14 px rows; rows are 12 px in 8 px text.
- `docs/STYLE.md`, `art/kit/*` (palette, GIMP palette, README, checklist), `tests/core/test_svg_art.gd`: the Arc ramp (40 colours).
- `art/manifest.json`, `content/story/lore/components/lightning.json`, `content/spells/combos.json` (two rows: the combo test needs two per component), `content/spells/README.md`.
- Tests and shots: `test_combos.gd`'s COMPONENTS, `test_validators.gd`, `test_svg_art.gd` (counts), `test_projectile_shapes.gd`, `test_combat_statuses.gd`, `test_quick.gd` (11 rows), `shot_element_shots.gd`, `shot_statuses.gd`.

## 2026-10-25: Spell visuals, reactions and statuses (#244, part 3)

A reaction is now a burst bigger than a plain hit: a ring that opens next to the flash, a particle burst 2.2 times the size, a hit-stop of 0.09 s (a death's is 0.07) and more shake, scaled per reaction by `reaction_power` (Shatter 1.3 the most, Stillness 0.9 the least), with the sound `reaction_<id>` as before. An enemy with burn, chill, freeze, shock or hex wears a tinted copy of its `Sprite` (orange pulse, steady pale blue, solid white-blue, a yellow blink with a one-pixel jitter, a slow purple pulse), read from `Combat.status_left` every 0.1 s and gone when the status ends; the hit flash covers it for its moment. Data is in the preset (`status_looks`, `status_order`). Sheet: `shot_statuses`; clips `reaction_*`. Tests: `test_feel_reaction_bursts.gd`. The reaction sounds themselves are still not in `audio/` (silent by design).

A rune is a diamond that swells and settles while it waits, an orbit shot two beads that turn and breathe (`shot_core.gdshader` kinds 9 and 10, animated from `sim_time`; the manager writes them for every player rune and orbit shot, whatever the element). Shaped bodies are drawn smaller (`SHAPE_SCALE` 0.62) after the owner's note that the new visuals were too big. Elements still show their colour (core) and their shape; the trail marks are the type particles. Tests: `tests/core/test_projectile_shapes.gd`. Sheet: `shot_form_bodies`; clips `form_rune`, `form_orbit`, `form_bolt`. More animation for combined spells is in the backlog.

## 2026-10-25: Spell visuals, forms (#244, part 2)

Bolt, beam, nova, rune and orbit each have a cast flash on the wizard (streak, long flare, ring burst, rune and orbit gathering in), a flare where they end or hit (beam stretched along the beam, nova and rune rings, orbit tick) and a beam that opens wide and thins. Data is `cast_looks` and `impact_looks` in the preset; an enemy's shot always ends in an ember mark on its corner.
